Evidence: `TestOpenAICompletionsEmptyToolCallDoesNotWedgeReplay` (`ai/openai_empty_tool_call_test.go`) streams the malformed call from a mock server, replays the transcript and checks the second request. `TestReplayOmitsUnreplayableToolCallsOnEveryProviderPath` and `TestStreamedToolCallsWithoutIDGetDistinctIDsAcrossTurns` (`ai/unreplayable_tool_call_test.go`) check a stored id-less named call, a blank-named call and a call with neither on the Completions/Responses, Anthropic and Google/Bedrock replay paths, and that generated ids do not repeat across turns. Mutating the id generation, the call filter or the result filter fails them. `TestOpenAICompletionsReplayOmitsStoredToolCallsWithoutIDOrNameReviewed` and `TestOpenAICompletionsGeneratedToolCallIDsAreUnique` (`ai/openai_empty_tool_call_reviewer_test.go`, from rev-fix-pi-bugs-ai-1) check the same rules through `ConvertCompletionsMessages` and a mock stream, including that a `requiresAssistantAfterToolResult` target gets no bridge message after an all-omitted result group.

Parity allowance: Pi 1.4.0 reproduces the 400, so no paired scenario can assert the fixed behavior. The Pi response oracle (`compareResponses` in `ai/response_oracle_support_test.go`) blanks PiG's generated `<name>_<ms>_<counter>` ids where Pi stores `""`, for every API except Google, whose adapters generate the same form in Pi (`google-generative-ai.ts:195-200`) so both sides carry one.

Remove when: Pi fills the id at stream end and omits unreplayable calls on replay.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:3c00972de63d5aba

## D121 The Anthropic SSE reader holds back a carriage return that ends a chunk (pi-mono #10390)

What: Pi's `consumeLine` (`anthropic-messages.ts:454-466`) ends a line at a `\r` that is the last character of a network chunk. The `\n` of a CRLF split that way then arrives as an empty line, flushes the record without its `data`, and the turn aborts with `Could not parse Anthropic SSE event message_start: Unexpected end of JSON input; data=`. PiG waits for the next chunk before it ends a line at a trailing `\r`; at the end of the stream the `\r` ends its line as before.

Why: where a proxy flushes CR and LF separately, every turn through it fails. A CRLF is one line break in the SSE standard.

Scope: `anthropicSSEReader` only (Anthropic Messages and the providers that use it). The OpenAI-compatible decoder already holds the `\r` back. Chunks without a split CRLF decode exactly as Pi does.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling "fix every confirmed bug now, ported from Pi or not".

Call-site markers: `ai/anthropic_stream.go` (`anthropicConsumeLine`).

Evidence: `TestAnthropicStreamSurvivesCRLFSplitAcrossChunks` (`ai/anthropic_sse_crlf_split_test.go`, whole provider path, every CR and LF in its own chunk) and the `carriage return at a chunk end` rows of `TestAnthropicSSEReaderLineDecoding`; removing the hold-back fails them.

Parity allowance: Pi 1.4.0 aborts on this input (reproduced on the previous pin with a mock that flushes `\r` and `\n` separately; the Anthropic SSE reader is unchanged in Pi 1.4.0), so no paired scenario asserts it.

Remove when: Pi holds back a trailing `\r` in `consumeLine`.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:24385b985637ffb1

## D122 OpenAI Completions keeps a signature-only `reasoning.text` delta (pi-mono #9534)

What: OpenRouter streams the signature of an Anthropic `reasoning.text` block in a later delta that has `signature`, `type`, `format` and `index` but no `text`. Pi's `isOpenAIReasoningDetail` (`openai-completions.ts:144-148`) requires `typeof detail.text === "string"`, so it drops the delta, and the `reasoning_details` Pi replays after a tool call lose the signature. PiG accepts a `reasoning.text` detail without `text` when its `signature` is a string, merges the signature into the preceding `reasoning.text` detail, and replays it. A `reasoning.text` detail with neither `text` nor a string `signature` is still dropped.

Why: OpenRouter documents that the original reasoning blocks, signatures included, must be passed back when a conversation continues after a tool call.

Scope: `decodeOpenAIReasoningDetail` in `ai/openai.go`, used by the streaming path, the `thinkingSignature` replay parser and the legacy-detail parser. Chunks that carry `text` decode as in Pi.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling "fix every confirmed bug now, ported from Pi or not".

Call-site markers: `ai/openai.go` (`decodeOpenAIReasoningDetail`).

Evidence: `TestOpenAICompletionsKeepsSignatureOnlyReasoningText` (`ai/openai_reasoning_signature_only_test.go`) streams the text delta and the signature delta, checks the stored `thinkingSignature` and the replayed `reasoning_details`; disabling the acceptance fails it.

Parity allowance: Pi 1.4.0 drops the delta, so no paired scenario asserts the fixed behavior.

Remove when: Pi keeps signature-only `reasoning.text` deltas.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:86583d1e7a5e00a1

## D128 OpenRouter image models that need the Images API are retried through `/images` (pi-mono #10652)

What: for some OpenRouter image generation models (the report names `openai/gpt-image-2.5-flare`) `chat/completions` answers `404 <model> is an image generation model and cannot be used with the chat/completions endpoint. Use the /api/v1/images endpoint instead.` Pi 1.4.0 returns `stopReason: "error"` with no images. When PiG receives that refusal it sends the request once to `POST <baseUrl>/images` with `{model, prompt, input_references}` (the text input joined by newlines, the image input as data-URL `image_url` parts) and reads `data[].b64_json` (`media_type` or `image/png`) and `usage` into the result, priced at the model's flat rates as the chat path is. Any other failure, and every model that `chat/completions` accepts, keeps Pi's single chat request and error.

Why: the refusal is OpenRouter's own statement of which endpoint serves the model; the catalog carries no field that tells the two apart (FLUX and Gemini image models work through chat completions), so the endpoint is discovered from the response.

Scope: `GenerateImagesOpenRouter` (`ai/openrouter_images.go`, `ai/openrouter_images_api.go`). The payload hook runs for each request and sees that request's own payload, as in D37 and Pi's Anthropic strict tool fallback. `onResponse` runs once, for the 2xx `/images` response: the refused chat request never resolves `withResponse()`.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling "fix every confirmed bug now, ported from Pi or not".

Call-site markers: `ai/openrouter_images.go` (`generateOpenRouterImages`).

Evidence: `TestOpenRouterImagesUsesTheImagesAPIWhenChatCompletionsRefusesTheModel` and `TestOpenRouterImagesOtherFailuresDoNotUseTheImagesAPI` (`ai/openrouter_images_api_endpoint_test.go`); the first fails with the branch disabled. `TestOpenRouterImagesAPIRequestRunsTheHooksAndJoinsTheTextInput` and `TestOpenRouterImagesAPIFallbackIsNarrowAndRunsOnce` pin the hooks, the prompt join, the status and text filter and the single retry. The response shape follows OpenRouter's Images API reference (`data[].b64_json`, `media_type`, `usage`).

Parity allowance: Pi 1.4.0 returns the 404 error; no paired scenario can reproduce OpenRouter's refusal.

Remove when: Pi sends these models to the Images API.

Pin re-check: owner HOLD 2026-10-10. The code stays. At every pin move, re-check Pi main for a fix of #10652 (OpenRouter image models refused by `chat/completions`): if Pi ships one, port it exactly and delete this record; if Pi picks a different approach (catalog flag, endpoint field), align PiG to it and reclassify.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:1f5f0daa9e23efac

## D129 A Responses function call streamed with an empty call_id no longer wedges the session (pi-mono #10299)

What: D120's Responses counterpart. Pi stores a streamed `function_call` or `custom_tool_call` under the id `${item.call_id}|${item.id}` (`openai-responses-shared.ts` `createSlot`). An absent `call_id` is stored as `undefined|<item id>` and `null` as `null|<item id>`; PiG stores the same ids, and both replay them. An empty `call_id` is stored as `|<item id>`, and its replay sends `call_id: ""` for the call and for its output, which the API rejects on every later request. PiG gives a call streamed with an empty `call_id` the generated `<name>_<epoch ms>_<counter>` call id of D120 (`<generated>|<item id>`), and on the Responses replay a stored id whose call part is still empty after ID normalization (`|`-prefixed) is omitted with the tool results that name it, as D120 omits an empty id. Other targets replay such a call as Pi does: Chat Completions normalizes `|fc_1` to `_fc_1`, Anthropic, Bedrock and Mistral normalize the whole id, and Gemini sends it unchanged.

Why: the same fault and fix shape as D120; the Responses id has two parts, so an empty call part is not an empty id.

Scope: `ai/openai_responses.go` (`responsesStreamedToolCallID` on the stream, `omitEmptyResponsesCallIDs` in `prepareResponsesTranscript`, which every Responses-family converter uses). A call with a non-empty, absent or null `call_id` is unchanged.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling "fix every confirmed bug now, ported from Pi or not".

Call-site markers: `ai/openai_responses.go` (`responsesStreamedToolCallID`, `omitEmptyResponsesCallIDs`).

Evidence: `TestResponsesToolCallWithoutCallIDDoesNotWedgeReplay` (`ai/openai_responses_idless_tool_call_test.go`); red before the fix (`function_call` and `function_call_output` replayed with `call_id` ""); disabling either half fails it. `TestResponsesEmptyCallIDOmissionIsConfinedToTheResponsesReplay` checks that Chat Completions and Gemini targets keep the call and its result, as Pi does. `TestOpenAIResponsesToolCallIDPartsMatchPi` and `TestStoredEmptyCallPartReplaysToOtherTargetsAsPi` (`ai/openai_responses_call_id_oracle_test.go`) compare the stored ids for absent and null `call_id` and `id` members, and the Chat Completions and Gemini replay payloads, with the pinned pi-ai (rev-fix-pi-bugs-ai-4).

Parity allowance: Pi 1.4.0 replays the empty `call_id`, so no paired scenario can assert the fixed behavior.

Remove when: Pi fills the id at stream end and omits unreplayable calls on replay, for the Responses id form too.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:1e8ba706aee023dc

## D130 A managed-effort Anthropic request sends each managed field only with its beta (Pi #10759)

What: for a model with `compat.supportsMidConvoEffort`, Pi's `buildParams` (`anthropic-messages.ts:1154-1164`, `:1235-1241`) always sends `thinking.block_binding` and the `role: "system"` `output_config` effort markers. An explicit `anthropic-beta` header, from `model.headers` or the request headers, replaces the computed betas, and `null` removes them (`getBetaFeatures`, `:1080-1103`; pinned by `anthropic-auth-token.test.ts`). The override drops `thinking-binding-controls-2026-08-01` and `mid-conversation-output-config-2026-07-01`, but the body still uses them, so the API returns 400 (`messages.1.output_config: Extra inputs are not permitted`, or `thinking.adaptive.block_binding: Extra inputs are not permitted` without the markers). PiG keeps the override semantics and derives the managed fields from the betas it sends. `block_binding` goes only with `thinking-binding-controls-2026-08-01`. The markers go only with `mid-conversation-output-config-2026-07-01`. Without the markers, the active effort is the top-level `output_config.effort` instead of `high`. Without an override, or with an override that lists both betas, the request is Pi's.

Why: the override is how a user adds a beta, and on these models every such request fails. The issue reports the 400s from the live API for claude-opus-5-5.

Scope: `buildParams` and `applySampling` in `ai/anthropic.go`, the Anthropic Messages path only. The `tool_addition` blocks of native tool changes without `inline-tools-2026-09-15` may fail the same way. The issue did not test that and PiG leaves it as Pi has it.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, lead assignment of Pi #10759 under the owner ruling "fix every confirmed bug now, ported from Pi or not".

Call-site markers: `ai/anthropic.go` (`buildParams`, `applySampling`).

Evidence: `TestAnthropicManagedEffortBodyFollowsTheBetasSent` (`ai/anthropic_beta_override_test.go`, catalog `claude-opus-5-5` through the provider's HTTP path) covers seven cases, each with an API key and with an OAuth token: the computed betas, a model-header override, a null request header, an override listing both betas, each beta alone, and betas whose names only start with the managed names. The first six failed in four cases per credential before the fix. Matching a managed beta as a substring of the header fails the last case. Sending `block_binding` regardless, deriving the markers from the effort alone, or keeping the top-level effort at `high` each fail four cases per credential, the last case among them.

Parity allowance: Pi 1.4.0 sends the managed fields regardless, and only the live API rejects the request, so no hermetic paired scenario asserts it.

Remove when: Pi derives `block_binding` and the mid-conversation markers from the betas it sends.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:aeddd7ea5c3c3ee0

## D131 A stalled SSE stream that still sends keepalives ends after the HTTP idle timeout (pi-mono #10761, #10762)

What: Pi's `httpIdleTimeoutMs` (default 300000) is a byte-level body timeout. A gateway that silently loses the generation but keeps the connection alive with comment lines (`: keepalive`) or `ping` events resets that timer for ever, so a headless `pi -p` run parks without an error or a retry. PiG measures the same timeout on semantic progress for every `text/event-stream` response of the provider HTTP clients: the timer restarts when a `data:` line of an event other than `ping` arrives. A reasoning or thinking event also makes keepalives count: while the last event with data was a reasoning event (Anthropic `thinking`, `thinking_delta`, `signature_delta` and `redacted_thinking`, OpenAI `reasoning` items and `response.reasoning*` events, OpenRouter, DeepSeek and Mistral `reasoning`, `reasoning_content`, `reasoning_text` and `reasoning_details`, Gemini `thought` parts), comment lines and `ping` events restart the timer too, because a model that reasons silently behind a gateway has not stalled. Any later event that is not about reasoning ends that state. When it expires the response body fails with `SSE Event Timeout Error: no event for <N>ms`, which the provider reports like any body error (`terminated`, with that cause) so the agent's retry policy applies. The timeout is the configured `httpIdleTimeoutMs`; `0` disables it, as it disables the byte-level timeout. A stream whose events arrive within the timeout is never cut off, however long it runs.

Why: a reasonable user expects a stalled stream to end with an error, and a long silent reasoning turn is not a stall (owner sign-off 2026-10-10). The default equals the existing setting, so no new setting or literal exists. A reasoning model that emits no event at all before its answer (for example Chat Completions reasoning that never streams) is still cut after `httpIdleTimeoutMs` of keepalives, because nothing marks it as reasoning.

Scope: `ai/sse_event_idle.go` (`sseIdleTransport`), installed in `newStreamingHTTPClient` and `streamingHTTPClientNoRetry` (`ai/http_transport.go`): the OpenAI Completions and Responses, Anthropic, Google and Mistral streams that use those clients. Bedrock and the Codex WebSocket keep their own transports. A caller-supplied fetch function is not wrapped.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, lead ruling "fix it ... idle measured on semantic progress (events), default equal to httpIdleTimeoutMs"; owner sign-off 2026-10-10: reasoning and thinking events count as progress, so silent long reasoning behind keepalives does not fail.

Call-site markers: `ai/sse_event_idle.go`.

Evidence: `TestSSEStreamWithOnlyKeepalivesEndsAfterTheIdleTimeout` (comment keepalives and ping events), `TestSSEStreamWithSlowEventsIsNotCutOff`, `TestSSEKeepalivesAfterAReasoningEventDoNotEndTheStream` (900 ms of keepalives behind a reasoning event, three times the timeout, still finishes), `TestSSEKeepalivesAfterANonReasoningEventStillEndTheStream` (after text, after reasoning then text, after a role chunk and after a reasoning token tally the stream still ends) and `TestSSEIdleTrackerRecognizesReasoningEvents` (each provider shape, with and without spaces; a text delta that says "thinking" and Responses' reasoning config echo do not count) (`ai/sse_event_idle_test.go`). With the transport disabled the first test never ends (it fails at the package timeout); a mutant that counts `ping` events as progress also hangs it.

Parity allowance: Pi 1.4.0 has no event-level timeout; no paired scenario can hold a stream open with keepalives.

Remove when: Pi ends a stream that sends no event for `httpIdleTimeoutMs`.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:9b641ac4fb1eeb5f

## D132 Session directories are created with mode 0700 (pi-mono #10770)

What: Pi creates the sessions directory and each project's session directory with the umask default (`mkdirSync(..., { recursive: true })`, 0755 under a 022 umask), so any other user of the machine can list and read every conversation, tool output and secret a model saw. PiG creates every directory it makes for sessions with mode 0700: the agent directory, `sessions` and the per-project directory when they do not exist yet, on session creation, clone, fork-from-file, `/import` and the migration of sessions out of the agent root. A directory that already exists keeps its mode; session files keep the default mode and are protected by their directory.

Why: the sessions are private data, and the report calls the default a security problem.

Scope: `MkdirSessionDir` (`internal/codingagent/session_manager.go`), called by `SessionManager.Create`, `Clone` and `ForkFromFile`, by `/import` (`coding/session_import.go`) and by `migrateSessionsFromAgentRoot` (`internal/codingagent/migrations.go`). The HTML export directory (`session_export.go`) is not a session directory and is unchanged.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, lead ruling "fix (security) ... all four MkdirAll sites, red test".

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/session_manager.go`, `internal/codingagent/migrations.go`, `coding/session_import.go`.

Evidence: `TestSessionDirectoriesAreCreatedOwnerOnly` (`internal/codingagent/session_dir_mode_test.go`) creates a session through `Create`, `Clone` and `ForkFromFile` under a fresh agent directory and checks every created directory is 0700; it fails with Pi's 0755.

Parity allowance: no paired scenario inspects directory modes.

Remove when: Pi creates session directories with mode 0700.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:c16a7d174dd1fcae

## D134 One SSE line is bounded at Node's maximum string length

What: Pi's SSE readers (the OpenAI SDK's `LineDecoder`, the Google GenAI SDK reader and `iterateSseMessages` in `anthropic-messages.ts`) have no line cap of their own, but each one holds the line in a JavaScript string. Node cannot make a string longer than `buffer.constants.MAX_STRING_LENGTH` (536,870,888 UTF-16 code units on 64-bit V8), so a longer line ends Pi's stream with `Cannot create a string longer than 0x1fffffe8 characters` or `RangeError: Invalid string length`. PiG's shared SSE decoder (`ai/sse.go`, used by OpenAI Responses, Google and the Codex WebSocket path) ends the stream at the same bound in bytes, with `bufio.Scanner: token too long`. Before comb-audit row 4 it ended at 32 MiB; the lane's first fix removed every bound.

Why: Go strings have no runtime length limit, so without this bound a server that never ends a line grows the buffer until the process runs out of memory. Pi's process fails at this length instead.

Scope: `newSSEDecoder` in `ai/sse.go` (`sseLineLimit`). The error text differs from Pi's, and the bound counts bytes where Node counts UTF-16 code units. The Anthropic, Mistral and Codex SSE readers keep their own buffers and are not changed.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, lead review instruction for fix-pi-bugs-ai 58a029e731: "the SSE line-cap removal must still bound memory (cite Pi)". Number taken from the top of fix-pi-bugs-ai's block (D120-D134) by its reviewer rev-fix-pi-bugs-ai-2; the lead confirms or renumbers it.

Call-site markers: `ai/sse.go` (`sseLineLimit`).

Evidence: `TestSSELineLimitIsNodesMaxStringLength` (`ai/sse_long_line_test.go`) compares the bound with `require('node:buffer').constants.MAX_STRING_LENGTH` from the pinned Node. `TestSSEDecoderBoundsALine` lowers the bound and requires a longer line to end the stream with `bufio.ErrTooLong`; it fails when the decoder is given no bound. `TestSSEDecoderAcceptsALineLargerThan32MiB` keeps the comb-audit row 4 case.

Parity allowance: no paired scenario streams a 512 MiB line.

Remove when: Pi bounds an SSE line itself, or divergence-guard accepts Node runtime constants as upstream references so that `sseLineLimit` can cite `MAX_STRING_LENGTH` directly.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:5567d38ee10096d8

## D135 clearQueue keeps queued extension custom messages (Pi #9886)

What: Pi's `AgentSession.clearQueue()` returns the queued user-message text and then calls `agent.clearAllQueues()`, which also destroys the custom messages an extension queued with `sendMessage({ deliverAs: "followUp" | "steer" })` during a run. The extension never learns that its message was dropped. PiG's `Session.ClearQueue` (RPC `clear_queue`, SDK) and the interactive "restore queued messages to the editor" action remove and report only the queued user messages. Queued custom messages stay queued and run with the active run. After the interactive restore with abort (Escape), the run stops and the kept custom messages run with the next run, as any queued message does after an abort in Pi.

Why: A host that implements "edit queued messages" as clear and replay loses the extension's background notification, and the parent model is never woken. The issue thread (reporter and a contributor, no maintainer decision) proposes keeping extension messages queued; that proposal makes it an opt-in `clearQueue({ userMessagesOnly: true })` used by the TUI, while PiG applies it to every `ClearQueue` caller, including RPC `clear_queue`.

Scope: `Session.ClearQueue`, `agent.Agent.ClearQueuedUserMessages` and the interactive restore action. `agent.ClearAllQueues` keeps Pi's behavior.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling that every confirmed Pi bug is fixed now, ported from Pi or not.

Call-site markers: `coding/session.go` (`ClearQueue`), `agent/agent.go` (`ClearQueuedUserMessages`), `internal/codingagent/interactive_input.go` (`restoreQueuedMessagesToEditor`).

Evidence: `TestClearQueueKeepsExtensionCustomMessages` in `coding/session_pi_bugs_test.go` streams a run, queues a custom message with each `deliverAs`, calls `ClearQueue` and requires the custom message in the transcript. It fails with the former `ClearAllQueues` call. `TestClearUserMessagesKeepsCustomMessagesInOrder` in `agent/queue_clear_user_test.go` pins the queue operation. `TestRestoreQueuedMessagesToEditorKeepsQueuedCustomMessages` in `internal/codingagent/restore_queue_custom_test.go` fails when the interactive restore calls `ClearAllQueues`.

Parity allowance: none; no paired scenario clears a queue that holds a custom message.

Remove when: Pi's `clearQueue` stops destroying custom messages it does not return.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:d2932a36c0ccf36c

## D136 A context edit after the usage anchor keeps the anchor (Pi #10287)

What: Pi's `estimateProjectedContextTokens` discards the last assistant usage whenever any `context_edit` follows it on the branch. Omitting a zero-usage failed retry attempt (the edit `_omitRecoveryAttempt` appends) therefore replaces the usage total by an estimate of the whole history, and the context indicator and the compaction check jump (42k to 330k tokens in the report). PiG keeps the anchor when every later edit targets an entry after it, because the trailing estimate already counts the projected content of those entries. An edit of the anchor or of earlier input, and any later compaction, still discard it.

Why: The reported jump triggers needless compaction after a retryable network error.

Scope: `compaction.EstimateProjectedContextTokens`, which feeds `Session.ContextUsage`, the threshold check and the compaction preparation's `tokensBefore`.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling that every confirmed Pi bug is fixed now, ported from Pi or not.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/compaction/estimate.go` (`EstimateProjectedContextTokens`).

Evidence: `TestEstimateKeepsUsageAnchorWhenARetryOmissionEditsOnlyTheTrailingSuffix`, `TestEstimateKeepsUsageAnchorWhenTrailingInputIsReplaced` and `TestEstimateDropsUsageAnchorWhenTheAnchorOrEarlierInputIsEdited` in `internal/codingagent/compaction/context_edit_retry_anchor_test.go`, and `TestContextUsageSurvivesAnOmittedFailedRetryAttempt` in `coding/session_pi_bugs_test.go`; the first, second and fourth fail on the former rule. The Pi-differential compaction oracle in `internal/codingagent/compaction/compaction_oracle_test.go` requires, for each generated case where an edit follows an assistant entry and `tokensBefore` differs, that PiG's `tokensBefore` equal Pi's own usage-anchored `estimateContextTokens` total, compares every other field, and fails when no case exercises the anchor.

Parity allowance: `prep.tokensBefore` in `compaction_oracle_test.go` for sessions with an edit after an assistant entry, bound to Pi's anchored estimate.

Remove when: Pi keeps the usage anchor across an edit that only touches later entries.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:0b9db4bafa4f02aa

## D137 A request carries at most the model's maxPerRequest images (Pi #10162)

What: Pi reads `inputLimits.images.maxPerRequest` from the catalog and from `models.json` and then ignores it, so a long run that reads many images sends every image on each request and ends with a provider 400 such as `Too many images in request: 31 > 30`. PiG replaces the oldest images of a request beyond the model's `maxPerRequest` with the text `[image omitted: this model accepts at most N images per request]` and keeps the newest N. The stored session is unchanged.

Why: The run stops on a provider error that the model's own metadata predicts. A model without a configured limit is unchanged, so the opencode-go models in the report need `inputLimits.images.maxPerRequest` in `models.json`; PiG does not learn a limit from the provider error text. A Pi maintainer lists compaction, dropping the oldest images and rejecting as open options and has not chosen one; each changes the prompt prefix. Once a run exceeds the limit, every request that adds an image moves the omitted set, so the provider's prompt cache misses from the oldest kept image onward. Catalog models with a limit, such as Anthropic's (100 or 600), are affected without configuration.

Scope: user and tool-result images in the provider request of an agent run (`agent.limitImagesPerRequest`).

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling that every confirmed Pi bug is fixed now, ported from Pi or not.

Call-site markers: `agent/transform.go` (`limitImagesPerRequest`), `agent/agent_loop.go` (the call after `TransformLLMMessages`).

Evidence: `TestLimitImagesPerRequestKeepsTheNewestImages` in `agent/image_limit_test.go` and `TestPromptSendsAtMostTheModelsImagesPerRequest` in `coding/session_pi_bugs_test.go`; the Session test fails without the call in `agent_loop.go` (5 images sent, limit 3).

Parity allowance: none; no paired scenario sets `maxPerRequest`.

Remove when: Pi enforces `inputLimits.images.maxPerRequest` on provider requests.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:3416434428618a25

## D138 session_compact custom messages reach the overflow retry (Pi #9051)

What: A `session_compact` handler that publishes passive context with `sendMessage({ triggerTurn: false })` during an overflow recovery has its message deferred to the end of the active run, so the immediate retry runs without it. PiG appends the deferred custom messages to the session once the overflow compaction has finished and before the interrupted turn retries. The recovered turn is omitted first, so no append lands between a tool call and its result.

Why: An extension that republishes exact state after compaction loses it for the one request that needs it. Pi PR #9738 makes the same flush (closed, unmerged).

Scope: `Session.autoCompactAndDecide` with `willRetry`. Threshold compaction and manual compaction keep Pi's deferral.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling that every confirmed Pi bug is fixed now, ported from Pi or not.

Call-site markers: `coding/session.go` (`autoCompactAndDecide`).

Evidence: `TestOverflowRetrySeesCustomMessagesPublishedBySessionCompact` in `coding/session_pi_bugs_test.go` runs two prompts, answers the second with a context-overflow error and requires the marker in the retry request; it fails without the flush.

Parity allowance: none.

Remove when: Pi flushes deferred custom messages before an overflow retry.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:68ff451569dd11a6

## D142 Settlement keeps idle waiters pending and abort discards deferred prompts (Pi #10704, #10705)

What: Pi 1.4.0 resolves a `waitForIdle()` waiter that was registered during a run as soon as the compaction an `agent_settled` handler awaits has finished, before the prompt the handler deferred has run (#10704). Pi's `abort()` called from a public `agent_settled` subscriber resolves, but a continuation an extension had already deferred still starts a provider request (#10705). PiG keeps a waiter that registered while the Session was busy pending until the `agent_settled` handlers and every prompt they deferred have finished. `waitForIdle()` on an idle Session still returns at once, so a handler can call it. An abort requested while settlement is in progress discards the prompts already deferred. Manual compaction's own abort keeps them, as Pi's `compact()` aborts only the run it replaces.

Why: A host that waits for idle must not report completion while work is still queued, and a stopped Session must not start provider requests.

Scope: `Session.WaitForIdle`, `Session.RequestAbort`, `Session.Abort` and the deferred settled actions (`coding/session_run_state.go`). `agent_settled` dispatch also runs without the Session lock now, so a handler that awaits `ctx.compact()` no longer deadlocks (language mechanics: Pi's single event loop has no such lock). An abort while a deferred prompt runs also discards the prompts deferred behind it, and a waiter stays pending through every deferred prompt, including one whose own run settles before the next starts. Interactive mode drives its own `agent_settled` dispatch and deferred actions (`internal/codingagent/interactive_turn.go`) and is not covered: there a waiter and an abort behave as in Pi.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling that every confirmed Pi bug is fixed now, ported from Pi or not.

Call-site markers: `coding/session_run_state.go` (`WaitForIdle`, `requestAbort`).

Evidence: `TestWaitForIdleStaysPendingThroughSettledHandlerCompactionAndContinuation` (the #10704 order `request:1 compact-complete continuation-queued request:2 idle-resolved`; it hung before the lock change and resolved early before the waiter change) and `TestAbortFromAgentSettledSubscriberDiscardsQueuedContinuation` (the #10705 order) in `coding/session_settled_idle_test.go`. Both are mutation-checked. `TestWaitForIdleStaysPendingThroughEveryDeferredPrompt`, `TestAbortDuringADeferredContinuationDiscardsTheRest`, `TestCustomMessageRunSettledHandlerAwaitsCompaction` and `TestSettledHandlerCompactionKeepsTheContinuationQueuedBeforeIt` fail when the batch count, the epoch check, the custom-run unlock or compaction's deferred-keeping abort is removed.

Parity allowance: none.

Remove when: Pi keeps registered waiters pending through settlement and discards deferred prompts on abort.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:152a56f0507ff204

## D143 A device-attributes reply split across slow reads is not forwarded as input (Pi #10657)

What: when a terminal delivers a DA1 reply in several slow reads, Pi 1.4.0 forwards the leading fragment after its 150 ms fragment timeout and then emits the remaining digits, separators and final `c` as printable key input, so the composer fills with text such as `24;28;32;42c`. PiG ports the issue's reference fix: a buffered private CSI prefix (`ESC[?` and any digits and `;` after it) has no fragment timeout and waits for its final byte. The reassembled reply then takes Pi's path: a DA1 owed to the keyboard protocol query settles negotiation, and any other reply, such as the color query's DA1, is forwarded whole. Input that cannot continue the prefix releases the buffer as input first, as in Pi. A bare `ESC[` is still replayed as input after 150 ms, as Pi's tests require; while a DA reply is owed, PiG then absorbs for one second a continuation that extends it towards a Kitty flags or DA1 reply, so a `?`, digit or `;` typed in that second after a forwarded `ESC[` is consumed too.

Why: A reply to a terminal query is not user input.

Scope: `TerminalInput` negotiation buffering in `tui/terminal_input.go` (`setNegotiationBuffer`, `armOwedReplyTail`, `absorbOwedReplyTail`). A typed `ESC[?` prefix waits for the next key instead of 150 ms.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling that every confirmed Pi bug is fixed now, ported from Pi or not.

Call-site markers: `tui/terminal_input.go` (`setNegotiationBuffer`, `owedReplyTailTimeout`).

Evidence: `TestReassemblesDelayedDA1RepliesForKeyboardAndColorQueries` in `tui/terminal_split_reply_test.go` ports the reference fix's test: both DA1 replies split into the issue's four 8-byte chunks 200 ms apart produce no input until complete. `TestForwardTerminalConsumesAnOwedReplySplitAcrossSlowReads` drives the process terminal's input loop and requires exactly the typed key (and, for a split right after `ESC[`, the replayed `ESC[`). Both fail when the `ESC[?` prefix gets the fragment timeout back; the `ESC[` case fails without the absorber.

Parity allowance: none.

Remove when: Pi keeps a split private CSI reply buffered until its final byte.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:6190f3a25f5d172f

## D145 Nested Errors keep their name and message in codemode output (Pi #10650)

What: an Error's `name` and `message` are not enumerable, so Pi's codemode serializes a nested rejection as `{}`. `text(await Promise.allSettled([...]))`, console output and the returned value then show `"reason":{}` although the message is available in the VM. PiG serializes `text()`, console output and the returned value with a replacer that writes an Error as `{"name":...,"message":...}` followed by the own enumerable fields Pi already writes, such as `code` or `status`. A value whose prototype, `name` or `message` cannot be read is written as Pi writes it. Stacks are not written. Arguments a script passes to a tool, and an explicit `JSON.stringify` in the script, keep normal JavaScript semantics.

Why: The output is the only view the model gets of a failed nested call.

Scope: `codemode/assets/prelude.js` (`errorReplacer`, `serializeOutput`, `format`, `outputText`). The file no longer matches Pi's `PRELUDE_SOURCE` byte for byte; `codemode/assets/PROVENANCE.md` records both hashes and the refresh step reapplies the patch.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling that every confirmed Pi bug is fixed now, ported from Pi or not.

Call-site markers: `codemode/assets/prelude.js` (`errorReplacer`).

Evidence: `TestNestedToolErrorsKeepTheirMessageInSerializedOutput` in `codemode/nested_error_output_test.go`. It failed before the change with `"reason":{}` in both the output and the returned value. `TestNestedErrorsKeepTheirEnumerableFields` fails when the replacer drops the fields Pi writes. `TestNestedErrorsKeepTheirMessageInConsoleOutputAndMixedResults` in `codemode/nested_error_console_test.go` fails when console output or the returned value skips the replacer. `TestNestedErrorsWithUnreadableNamesSerializeAsPiDoes` in `codemode/nested_error_unreadable_test.go` fails when the replacer throws for a value Pi serializes.

Parity allowance: none.

Remove when: Pi's codemode serializes nested Errors with their name and message.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:a51e50b6b794c2c9

## D146 RPC stdin end answers a Session-replacing command that Pi leaves unanswered

What: Pi dispatches every stdin line at once (`rpc-mode.ts:833`), so `new_session`, `fork`, `clone`, `switch_session` and `export_html` answer after a later command of the same batch that is not a replacement (a `get_state`), in Pi and in PiG. When stdin ends while a replacement still awaits `createRuntime`, Pi exits at once (`rpc-mode.ts:826-828` calls `shutdown`, `:744-755`) and never answers the command. PiG finishes the replacement and writes its response before it shuts down. Measured on the previous pin (Pi 1.4.0's `rpc-mode.ts` is unchanged): `printf '{"id":"1","type":"new_session"}\n{"id":"2","type":"get_state"}\n' | pi --mode rpc` prints only the `get_state` response. An extension command in the same batch as a pending replacement runs after the replacement, as before, because PiG finishes the replacement first (Pi's replacement reaches its `session_shutdown` before such a command's handler).

Two replacements in flight together differ: Pi starts the second at once (`rpc-mode.ts:833`, `:456-463` `new_session`, `:625-635` `switch_session` and `fork`), and each acts on the Session that is current when it starts (`agent-session-runtime.ts:167-178` `teardownCurrent`, `:180-185` `apply`, `:187-194` `finishSessionReplacement`, `:226-259` `newSession`). Measured on Pi 1.5.2 for `new_session` + `new_session`, `new_session` + `switch_session` and `fork` + `new_session`, in one write and 50 ms apart, with an extension that logs `session_start` and `session_shutdown`: (1) in one write the outgoing Session gets `session_shutdown` twice (`:212`, `:245`, once per command) and the first replacement's Session none, because `apply` overwrote it and nothing disposes it; (2) 50 ms apart, while the first replacement's `session_start` handler runs, that Session gets `session_shutdown` and is disposed under the handler (`agent-session.ts:1477-1488`), and a handler that uses its captured `pi` afterwards gets the `extension_error` `This extension ctx is stale after session replacement or reload.` (`extensions/runner.ts:729-740`); (3) when the first replacement's `session_start` handler is the longer one, Pi answers the second command first. Pi's outcome is the same in every run of `TestPiOverlappingReplacementsD146`. The active Session and the Session files afterwards are the same as PiG's. PiG runs the second replacement after the first applied (the wait on `previous` in `awaitReplacement`): each Session gets one `session_shutdown`, one `session_start` each, no `extension_error`, and the answers come in the order sent.

Why: A script that pipes commands and closes stdin gets no answer to its replacement command from Pi, and cannot tell a failure from a drop. This is a Pi bug under the owner's rule that confirmed bugs are fixed whether or not Pi has them. Running the second of two replacements after the first applied is a CURE of a Pi defect under the owner's rule of 2026-11-13T22:52:04Z: it breaks no Pi-compatible use, because the final Session and the Session files are the same. A client can observe only the order of the two responses when the first replacement's `session_start` handler is the longer one (PiG: request order, Pi: the second first) and the extension events (one `session_shutdown` per Session).

Pi bug: Pi's second Session-replacing command overlaps the first (`rpc-mode.ts:833`, `agent-session-runtime.ts:167-185`, `:226-259`) and acts on whichever Session is current, so a Session gets `session_shutdown` twice, or none, or while its `session_start` handler runs, and that handler's captured context is then stale. Not reported upstream: no upstream issue records it. A draft for the owner to file is in `docs/parity/gap-closure/ledger-coding-agent-pi-issue-drafts.md` (D146).

Scope: `coding/cli/rpc_mode.go` (`awaitReplacement`, `finishReplacements`, the stdin-end wait). Replacements of one batch chain through `rpcAwaitInOrder`: each starts after the previous applied and answers after it. Pi starts them at once (`rpc-mode.ts:456-463`, `agent-session-runtime.ts:226-259`): its second replacement shuts down the Session the first has not yet applied or bound, so Pi sends `session_shutdown` twice to the outgoing Session and none to the first replacement's, or one while that Session's `session_start` handler still runs (stale ctx, `extension_error`), and answers the second first when the first's handlers run longer. PiG delivers each event once and answers in request order; the final Session is the same. A fork or clone requested while an earlier replacement is pending reads its entry from the Session that was current when it was requested (`coding.WithForkSession`, `Session.ValidateFork`), so it succeeds as in Pi, and a failed check answers before that replacement does. `export_html` answers at stdin end in Pi and in PiG.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling that every confirmed Pi bug is fixed now, ported from Pi or not. Lead question for the stdin-end drop: `questions/comb-audit.md`, row 58. For replacements in flight together: the owner's rule of 2026-11-13T22:52:04Z ("fix all bugs": PiG's own, and Pi bugs PiG can cure without breaking Pi compatibility), the form of D632.
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern; a cure of a Pi defect under the owner's fix-all-bugs rule of 2026-11-13T22:52:04Z

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/rpc_mode.go` (`replacementWG`, the stdin-end wait).

Evidence: `TestRPCSessionReplacementUsesDefaultSessionDir`, `TestRPCModeSessionReplacementAndExport` (commands sent one at a time) and `TestRPCInputEndAfterQueuedReplacementWithPendingShutdownExitsZero`. The interleaving is `TestRPCSessionReplacementAnswersAfterALaterCommandOfItsBatch`, `TestRPCReplacementsOfOneBatchRunInRequestOrder` (switch_session, new_session, new_session, get_state answers 4,1,2,3), `TestRPCCloneAfterNewSessionOfOneBatchUsesTheRequestingSession`, `TestRPCReplacementStartsAfterItsBatchWasDispatched` (red when the start-after-dispatch wait is removed) and scenario `55-rpc-replacement-concurrent-get-state`. Two replacements in flight: `TestPiOverlappingReplacementsD146` (a pin of the pinned Pi's behaviour through the Pi binary: it goes red when Pi changes) and `TestRPCOverlappingReplacementsRunOneAfterTheOtherD146` (pig), for `new_session` + `new_session`, `new_session` + `switch_session` and `fork` + `new_session`, in one write and with the second written once the first replacement's `session_start` handler runs (held one second), with `coding/cli/testdata/d146-overlap-probe.mjs`. Mutation of the pig test: the `select` on `previous` before `replace` removed in `awaitReplacement`. It compiles and runs without a deadlock or a panic, and the test fails on its assertions in all twelve rows: with the second command written while the first handler runs, the first replacement's Session gets its `session_shutdown` under that handler (`ShutdownInHeldStart` true); in the one-write rows the outgoing Session's `session_shutdown` and the Sessions' `session_start` counts differ from the one-after-the-other run.

Parity allowance: two replacements of one batch, the first replacement's `session_start` handler the longer one: PiG answers them in the order sent where Pi answers the second first, and PiG sends one `session_shutdown` to each Session (Pi: twice to the outgoing Session and none to the first replacement's, or one while that Session's `session_start` handler still runs) and one `session_start` to each Session, as Pi does. Which Session is active afterwards and the Session files are the same.

Remove when: Pi answers a replacement command that is in flight when stdin ends. The part about replacements in flight together goes when Pi runs them one after the other (it starts every line at once at `rpc-mode.ts:833` and does not guard `AgentSessionRuntime` in Pi 1.5.2, and nothing upstream says it will): until then PiG keeps its order, which cannot be dropped without copying the double `session_shutdown`.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:637a3672de15efe8

## D147 Compactions requested in one input batch run one after another

What: Pi's `compact()` awaits `abort()` before it creates its abort controller, so two `compact` lines of one input batch both start, and neither aborts the other. For a Session that is too small both answer "Nothing to compact", and for a Session with enough history Pi compacts twice at the same time. PiG chains the compactions through `rpcAwaitInOrder`: each starts after the previous one settled. Both answer "Nothing to compact" for a small Session, as in Pi (measured on the previous pin, 6 of 6 runs; Pi 1.4.0's `compact()` is unchanged). For a Session with enough history Pi answers both compacts with success and writes two compaction entries (previous pin, 3 of 3 runs). PiG starts the second compaction after the first one wrote its entry, so the second answers "Already compacted" and the Session has one compaction entry.

Why: Concurrent compactions of one Session write overlapping compaction entries and cancel each other when the second claims the Session while the first starts (PiG answered "Compaction cancelled" in 2 of 3 runs).

Scope: `coding/cli/rpc_mode.go` (`RPCCommandCompact`, `compactResolved`).

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, lead order to route the compact race through the one ordering mechanism.

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/rpc_mode.go` (`compactResolved`).

Evidence: `TestRPCCompactsOfOneBatchDoNotCancelEachOther`, red before the change (10 runs).

Parity allowance: none.

Remove when: Pi serializes the compactions of one Session.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:1abb156d14f6e5e7

## D148 An aborted compaction answers one error; two Pi defects of overlapping compacts are not reproduced

What: Pi's replacement (`new_session`, `fork`, `clone`, `switch_session`) disposes the outgoing Session at its teardown, which aborts that Session's compaction (`agent-session.ts:1393-1399`) without waiting for it. A `compact` of the same batch yields before its summary request, so the teardown comes first: the compact fails with "This operation was aborted" (getAuth rejects with the signal's reason) and writes no compaction entry. A replacement that is refused or fails never reaches its teardown and leaves the compaction alone. PiG matches both: each compact of a batch waits after `compaction_start` until every replacement requested by the end of the batch tore down (which cancels it) or gave up. Where Pi's own code fails differently, PiG does not copy the failure. With `compact, compact, new_session` Pi answers the first compact with the TypeError "Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'signal')", because the second compact clears the shared abort controller, and answers 2, 1, 3. PiG answers both compacts with "This operation was aborted" in request order. An abort after the summary request started answers "Compaction cancelled" for every provider, where Pi's text depends on whether the provider reports the abort as a message or as an error. PiG also writes a `compaction_end` event with `aborted: true` before the aborted compact's error answer; Pi writes none, because the outgoing Session is already unsubscribed.

Why: Letting the compaction finish wrote an entry into the Session file the replacement then left, and cancelling at dispatch aborted a compaction whose replacement was refused. The TypeError and the answer order are defects of Pi's shared controller, not behavior a user can rely on. The abort text after the request started is the Session's one cancellation answer.

Scope: `coding/cli/rpc_mode.go` (`awaitReplacementDecisions`, `cancelCompactions`, the compact command), `coding/session.go` (`WithCompactionYield`, the answer of an aborted compaction), `coding/session_summarization_auth.go` (`prepareSummarizationRequest`).

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, lead order to match Pi for a compact and a replacement in one batch (review rounds 7 to 9).

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/rpc_mode.go` (`compactionCancels`), `coding/session.go` (`compact`).

Evidence: `TestRPCReplacementAbortsTheCompactionOfItsBatch` (new_session in both orders, clone, fork, two compacts; red without the yield), `TestRPCRefusedReplacementLeavesTheCompactionOfItsBatchAlone` (red when the compaction is cancelled at dispatch), `TestRPCReplacementKeepsTheNothingToCompactAnswer`, `TestCompactCancelsSynchronouslyFromStartEvent`. Pi on the previous pin (its compaction and replacement paths are unchanged in Pi 1.4.0), with test-faux and a 1 MB Session, writes 0 compaction entries for the first group and 1 for the refused group.

Parity allowance: none.

Remove when: Pi no longer shares one abort controller between concurrent compactions and reports an aborted compaction with one text.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:e53f21f9cd8c53fa

## D150 `--no-stdin` skips the startup read of piped stdin (Pi #10415)

What: PiG adds `--no-stdin`. It skips the startup read of piped stdin in print and interactive-redirected runs. Without the flag PiG reads stdin until end of input and merges it into the prompt, as Pi does.

Why: `pi -p hi < <(sleep 15)` never starts: `readPipedStdin` (`packages/coding-agent/src/main.ts:80-97`) waits for `end` on a pipe whose writer stays open, and the same happens in PiG. An idle open pipe cannot be told from a slow producer, so a timeout would drop real input; an explicit opt-out is the only fix that cannot lose piped input. Callers that keep an input pipe open (agent harnesses, CI wrappers) pass the flag. Pi has no fix: Pi #10415 has an unmerged `--no-stdin` proposal and no PR. Pi #5571 discusses the same hang.

Observable effect: `pig --no-stdin -p "prompt"` with an open stdin pipe runs the prompt; Pi hangs. Pi parses `--no-stdin` as an unknown extension flag.

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/args.go` (`Args.NoStdin`, the `--no-stdin` case), `coding/cli/main.go` (the `readPipedStdin` call site).

Locked by: `TestNoStdinStartsWithAnOpenInputPipe` (runs the binary with an open stdin pipe holding unread data), `TestParseArgsNoStdin` in `coding/cli/no_stdin_test.go`.

Parity allowance: none. No Pi scenario passes the flag.

Remove when: Pi accepts `--no-stdin` or otherwise lets a caller start with an open stdin pipe, or fixes #10415.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:301efba687331ecb

## D153 The bash truncation notice names the size of the last line, not the bytes after the final newline (Pi #10015)

What: when the last line of bash or PowerShell output is over the byte limit, PiG's `[Showing last 50.0KB of line N (line is <size>)...]` notice gives the size of that line. If the output ends with a newline, Pi's `getLastLineBytes` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/tools/output-accumulator.ts`) returns the bytes after the newline, so Pi's notice reads `line is 0B`.

Why: `node -e 'process.stdout.write("a".repeat(60000) + "\n")'` produces `[Showing last 50.0KB of line 1 (line is 0B)...]` in Pi (measured on the previous pin; the bash truncation notice is unchanged in Pi 1.4.0) and in PiG. The tail it shows belongs to line 1, which is 58.6KB. Pi has no fix (Pi #10015 is open, with no PR).

Observable effect: PiG prints `line is 58.6KB`; Pi prints `line is 0B`. Output without a trailing newline is the same in both.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/tools/output_accumulator.go` (`LastLineBytes`).

Locked by: `TestBashLongLastLineNoticeNamesTheLineSize` (the bash tool) and `TestOutputAccumulatorLastLineBytes` (chunk boundaries, blank last line, split multi-byte character) in `internal/codingagent/tools/last_line_bytes_test.go`.

Parity allowance: `test/parity/scenarios/tools/31-print-bash-long-last-line.toml` runs such a command (a last line over the limit that ends with a newline) and compares the whole tool result. It replaces only the clause `(line is 0B)` or `(line is 213.9KB)` with one token, and its `[diverge]` block requires `(line is 0B)` in Pi's output and `(line is 213.9KB)` in PiG's, so the pair fails the day Pi reports the size of the line. `tools/15-builtin-tool-wire-details` replaces the same clause inside its probes.

Remove when: Pi reports the size of the last counted line.

Owner decision 2026-11-17: group 3 re-approval (small edits: wording, citation, a widening that follows Pi or an approved record, or a claim checked against its code and test); checked by Claude and Tern
SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:a435c14a060051f9

## D156 MCP server `url` expands environment references (Pi #10654)

What: PiG expands the environment references of an MCP server's `url` (`$VAR`, `${VAR}`, `$$`) the way it resolves `headers` and `env` values. It validates the expanded URL when it loads `mcp.json` or registers a server, connects to it, and uses it as the OAuth server URL. A `url` that cannot be expanded now (a `!command`, or a variable that is unset or empty) is used as written, so a literal URL with a `$` keeps working. Pi uses the `url` as written in `validateMcpServerConfig` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/mcp-servers.ts`), `createDefaultTransport` and `McpServerConnection.oauthUrl` (`packages/coding-agent/src/extensions/mcp/runtime.ts`).

Why: `{"url": "${MY_VAR}"}` is rejected with `url must be an http or https URL` in Pi 1.4.0 and in PiG, while `"http://${HOST}/mcp"` is accepted and then connects to the literal string. The reporter asks for the URL to be resolved with the same logic as headers and env values. Pi #10654 is open with no PR.

Observable effect: the `/mcp` server menu adds a `connects to: <expanded url>` line under a `url` that expands to something else (owner-approved; the expansion can contain a secret from the environment, and the menu shows it). An entry whose `url` is only a reference loads in PiG and is rejected by Pi. An entry whose `url` contains `$$`, or `$NAME` or `${NAME}` with `NAME` set, connects to the expanded URL in PiG and to the literal text in Pi. The stored config and `getMcpServers` keep the URL as written, and the server list shows it as written.

Call-site markers: `coding/extension/mcp_servers.go` (`ExpandMcpURL`, `ValidateMcpServerConfig`), `coding/mcpext/runtime.go` (`CreateDefaultTransport`, `Connection.OAuthURL`), `coding/mcpext/manager.go` (`serverMenu`).

Locked by: `TestValidateMcpServerConfigChecksTheExpandedURL`, `TestExpandMcpURL` (`coding/extension/mcp_url_expansion_test.go`), `TestMCPConnectionsExpandTheServerURLReferences` (`coding/mcpext/connection_test.go`) and `TestMcpManagerServerMenuShowsTheExpandedURL` (`coding/mcpext/manager_test.go`).

Parity allowance: none. No paired scenario uses a reference in a `url`.

Remove when: Pi expands the `url` of an MCP server.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:b9db2af7fdfb73a6

## D157 PiG's startup additions stay quiet when the config root cannot be written

What: PiG prints no `pig: warning: extension SDK staging failed: ...` line and no `pig: warning: write Piglet verification cache: ...` line when the write into the config root fails with a read-only file system or a permission denial (`EROFS`, `EACCES`, `EPERM`). Every other failure of either write still warns. The documentation bundle is not named when it could not be written (D22). `pig diagnose` reports a read-only config root and what PiG does not write there, including that a signed Piglet Binary checks itself in full at every start. The owner chose this on 2026-10-09 for both warnings.

Why: a config root that is read-only (a Nix or container image home, a locked-down CI cache) failed these writes on every start, with up to four lines on stderr in every mode, including `-p`, json and rpc. Both writes are PiG additions with no Pi counterpart. Pi on the same root (measured on the previous pin; its settings lock and settings diagnostics are unchanged in Pi 1.4.0) warns on every start about its own settings lock (`Warning: Invalid settings file .../settings.json: EACCES: permission denied, mkdir '.../settings.json.lock'`, also for `--help`), and then crashes with an `EACCES` stack trace when it needs `auth.json` or the sessions directory. PiG keeps Pi's settings warning, which says the settings were not applied, and does not add its own warnings for a read-only deployment.

Observable effect: no extension SDK staging line and no verification cache line on a read-only config root. An unusable staged SDK still fails its build with the build's own error; a signed Piglet Binary still verifies itself in full at every start.

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/main.go` (`stageExtensionSDKsAtStartup`, `reportVerifyCacheError`), `coding/cli/diagnose.go`.

Locked by: `TestReadOnlyConfigRootError` and `TestVerifyCacheWarningIsQuietOnlyOnAReadOnlyConfigRoot` in `coding/cli/readonly_config_root_test.go`; `TestStartupSDKStageIsQuietOnAReadOnlyConfigRoot` in `coding/cli/startup_sdk_test.go`; `TestSignedPigletBinaryRunsWhenItsVerifyCacheCannotBeWritten` (real signed Binary: quiet on a read-only state directory, warns when a file blocks the cache); `TestReadOnlyConfigRootStartsQuietlyAndNamesOnlyDocsThatExist` (real binary); `TestDiagnoseReportsAReadOnlyConfigRoot`; `TestStartupSDKStageLockWaitIsBoundedAndVisible` keeps the warning for other failures.

Parity allowance: none. No Pi scenario has a read-only config root.

Remove when: Pi stops warning on every start of a read-only config root, or PiG writes neither the staged extension SDK nor the Piglet Binary verification cache at startup.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:6e814c19d3853c8d

## D158 A setting of the wrong JSON type is ignored (Pi 1.4.0 throws)

What: `decodeSettingsWire` drops a `settings.json` value whose JSON type does not match the setting and loads the rest of the file. `{"skills":5}`, `{"extensions":{}}`, `{"enabledModels":[5]}` and `{"enabledModels":"ab"}` start normally and apply the other settings. Pi 1.4.0 passes the raw value to code that expects an array of strings: `{"skills":5}` and `{"extensions":{}}` exit 1 with `TypeError: entries is not iterable` (`splitPatterns`, reached through `DefaultPackageManager.resolveLocalEntries`), `{"enabledModels":[5]}` exits 1 with `TypeError: pattern.includes is not a function` (`resolveModelScopeFromModels`), and `{"enabledModels":"ab"}` iterates the string and warns `No models match pattern "a"` and `"b"`.

Why: a start that crashes on a mistyped setting, or reads a string as a list of one-character patterns, helps nobody. The user keeps every other setting, and the mistyped one has no effect. Pi does not intend the crash: it validates nothing.

Observable effect: PiG exits 0 where Pi exits 1 or warns once per character, and prints one warning per settings file that held such values, naming each ignored setting and the type it expects (`Warning: Invalid settings file <path>: ignored settings of the wrong type: "skills" (expected an array of strings)`; an array that lost elements reads `"packages" (an element of the wrong type)`). Owner-approved on 2026-10-10: the warning, so a user is not left wondering why a setting had no effect.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/settings.go` (`decodeSettingsWire`, `noteIgnoredSettings`).

Locked by: `TestSettingsDropWrongTypedValuesAndKeepTheRest` and `TestSettingsWarnOnceNamingTheIgnoredSettingAndItsExpectedType` (`internal/codingagent/settings_wrong_type_test.go`).

Parity allowance: the Pi settings-diagnostics oracle test (`TestSettingsFileDiagnosticsMatchPi`) skips PiG's ignored-settings warning. No paired scenario writes a wrong-typed setting.

Remove when: Pi validates the type of each setting, or the owner chooses to match Pi's crash.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:1069593c3e964b70

## D159 A single grapheme wider than the editor's wrap width stays one chunk instead of exhausting the stack

What: `wordWrapLine` (`packages/tui/src/components/editor.ts:123`) re-wraps a segment wider than `maxWidth` by calling itself on that segment. A segment that is one grapheme (a tab, which `visibleWidth` counts as 3, at a content width of 2 or less; a double-width character at width 1) is its own re-wrap input, so Pi recurses until `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded`. PiG's Go stack overflow is a fatal runtime error that no handler catches: the process dies. PiG keeps such a grapheme as one chunk that overflows the width, as the segment already does for cursor movement and editing, and re-wraps only segments of several graphemes (paste markers).

Why: typing a tab into an editor at a width of 3 columns crashed the whole program. Found by `FuzzEditorInputKeepsRenderValid`.

Scope: `wordWrapLine` in `tui/editor.go`. A segment of several graphemes, and any line that fits, wrap as in Pi.

Owner decision: 2026-10-10, owner ruling "fix every confirmed bug now, ported from Pi or not"; the lead allocated D159 the same night.

Call-site markers: `tui/editor.go` (`wordWrapLine`).

Evidence: `TestEditorWrapsOverWideGraphemeWithoutRecursion` (`tui/editor_wide_grapheme_test.go`) and the fuzz seed `tui/testdata/fuzz/FuzzEditorInputKeepsRenderValid/3ac102d681d9c167`.

Parity allowance: Pi 1.4.0's exported `wordWrapLine` throws `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` for `("a\tb", 2)` and `("日本", 1)` (measured against the installed `@earendil-works/pi-tui`), so no paired scenario can assert the fixed behavior.

Remove when: Pi stops re-wrapping a one-grapheme segment.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:22548648dfccd167

## D167 A history longer than the model's context window is summarized in chunks

What: when the messages a compaction summarizes, the summary its request carries, the instructions and the output the request asks for (the model's output limit) do not together fit the model's context window, PiG splits the messages into consecutive chunks, each the most messages that fit a request carrying the summary so far, and summarizes them in order. Each chunk's request carries the summary of the chunk before it in `<previous-summary>` and uses `UPDATE_SUMMARIZATION_PROMPT`, the prompt Pi uses when a compaction has a previous summary. The first chunk starts from the compaction's own previous summary, and the usage of every request is added. A chunk the provider still refuses as a context overflow (`ai.IsContextOverflow` on its error) is halved and retried until one message is left. A history that fits the window is one request, byte-identical to Pi's. When a compaction cuts inside a turn and the turn prefix does not fit by the same measure, all but the prefix's last window-sized part joins the history and is summarized by the chunked path; the turn-prefix request keeps the last part. A split turn whose prefix fits keeps Pi's two requests. `/tree` branch summarization keeps Pi's request for the newest entries that fit the window by the character estimate; when the provider still refuses it as a context overflow, PiG retries with the newest half of what it sent, never less than the newest message, until the provider accepts it or one message is left. A retry that would select no fewer messages ends with the provider's error.

Pi bug: `generateSummaryWithUsage` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/compaction/compaction.ts`) serializes every message of `messagesToSummarize` into one request and `serializeConversation` only caps tool results at 2000 characters. A session already longer than the model's window (a smaller-window model after a switch, or a long session on a 1M model) is refused with `prompt is too long: N tokens > W maximum`, and no later `/compact`, automatic compaction or overflow recovery can succeed. Pi (on the previous pin; its `compaction.ts` is unchanged in Pi 1.4.0) and the PiG integrate tip fail identically on the same session against the same mock endpoint (`prompt is too long: 12034 tokens > 6000 maximum`); Pi main has no chunking either. The owner hit it with `prompt is too long: 1,200,311 > 1,000,000`.

Scope: `GenerateSummaryWithUsageUsing` in `internal/codingagent/compaction/compaction.go` and `GenerateBranchSummary` in `internal/codingagent/compaction/branch_summarization.go`, and `Compact` for the turn prefix of a split turn, so manual `/compact`, automatic compaction, overflow recovery and both halves of a split-turn compaction. A turn prefix whose last part the provider still refuses is not halved; the refusal stays the error, as in Pi. A model without a known context window, a history that fits, and a single message longer than the window keep Pi's single request and its provider error. A request is sized by the four-characters-per-token estimate times the ratio of the provider's real tokens to that estimate on the session's latest assistant usage (between 1 and 2) times a margin of 1.05 (the request body's JSON framing, 2 to 3 percent, which the estimate does not count); a session without trusted usage keeps the first form: chunks of 0.75 x (window - twice the output limit - instructions) by the plain estimate; when the provider refuses a calibrated request as a context overflow the rest of the history is split by that first form.

Owner decision: 2026-10-11, owner approved the sign-off entry for the over-window compaction failure (lane fix-pi-bugs-ai); number assigned by the lead. The owner extended it to `/tree` branch summarization. The owner approved the calibrated request size conditionally (`OWNER-DECISIONS.md` 2026-11-15T00:25:17Z: only if it is a net positive without another regression).

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/compaction/compaction.go` (`GenerateSummaryWithUsageUsing`, `Compact`), `internal/codingagent/compaction/branch_summarization.go` (`GenerateBranchSummary`).

Evidence: `TestGenerateSummaryOfAHistoryLongerThanTheWindowIsChunked`, `TestGenerateSummaryHalvesAChunkTheProviderRefusesAsTooLong` and `TestGenerateSummaryOfOneMessageLongerThanTheWindowKeepsTheProvidersError` (`internal/codingagent/compaction/over_window_history_test.go`; red on the integrate tip with the Pi-identical overflow error), `TestGenerateSummaryOfAHistoryThatFitsTheWindowIsOneRequest`, `TestGenerateBranchSummaryHalvesARequestTheProviderRefusesAsTooLong`, `TestGenerateBranchSummaryOfABranchThatFitsIsOneRequest` and `TestGenerateBranchSummaryOfOneRefusedMessageKeepsTheProvidersError`, `TestCompactionOfASplitTurnLongerThanTheWindowIsChunked`, `TestCompactionOfASplitTurnThatFitsKeepsPisRequests`, `TestGenerateSummaryHalvesThreadTheSummary`, `TestGenerateSummaryChunksPoisonedHistory`, `TestGenerateBranchSummaryHalvingKeepsTheNewestMessage` and `TestGenerateBranchSummaryHalvingWithoutProgressKeepsTheProvidersError` (`internal/codingagent/compaction/over_window_review_test.go`), `TestCompactionOfAHistoryThatFitsWithItsOutputCapIsOneRequest`, `TestSplitOfAHistoryIsNeverMoreRequestsThanTheFirstFormAndNeverRefused`, `TestCompactionOfASessionWithoutUsageIsTheFirstFormOfTheSplit`, `TestCompactionOfACalibratedSessionSizesRequestsByItsRatio`, `TestCompactionAfterARefusalOfACalibratedRequestContinuesByTheFirstForm`, `TestCompactionOfAHistoryTheEstimateUndercountsHalvesAfterTheRefusal`, `TestSummaryPlanTokenRatio`, `TestPrepareCompactionMeasuresTheProviderTokenRatio` and `TestCompactionPreparationShowsOnlyPisFields`, and the 1500-seed `TestGenerateSummaryMatchesPi`, which still passes. An RPC `compact` of a 60-message session against a mock endpoint with a 6000-token window fails on Pi (previous pin; `compaction.ts` is unchanged in Pi 1.4.0) and on the integrate tip and succeeds with this change in five requests of at most 4008 estimated tokens.

Parity allowance: no paired scenario compacts a history longer than the model's window, and the oracle rows summarize histories that fit one request.

Remove when: Pi summarizes over-window histories itself (chunking or trimming the summary input).

Owner decision 2026-11-17: group 2 re-approval, the owner's dated word covers the current text; checked by Claude and Tern
SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:4e9e6e35b2870828

## D177 The calls of a parallel durable tool round start in unspecified order

What: In a parallel tool round (the default `toolExecution`, no call's tool requiring sequential execution) PiG runs each call's tool task as its own goroutine, so the calls start concurrently and the order of their first side effects within one round is unspecified. Pi 1.4.0's generation creates every tool task of the round in one commit and its single event loop runs each `execute` up to its first await in call order, so `harness-tools.test.ts:263` observes `["start a", "start b"]` exactly. Pi does not specify that order; it follows from running on one thread.

Why: Go has no single event loop; ordering the starts would need a gate that holds each call until every earlier call of its round has reached a wait, which couples the harness to the scheduler and to how the runtime parks goroutines. The owner chose to leave the start order unspecified (option b).

Scope: the start order of the tool calls of one parallel round in `durable/harness`. Unchanged: every call of a parallel round starts before any of them ends, the next model request carries the round's tool results in call order, and a sequential round (configured, or required by a tool) runs one call after another in call order.

Owner decision: 2026-10-13, owner approved option b (record the start order as unspecified rather than gate the starts), relayed by DurableLead; number assigned by DurableLead.

Call-site markers: `durable/harness/generation.go` (`createToolTask` in the generation's tool-round commit).

Evidence: `TestToolRound` "runs a round in parallel by default and sequentially when configured or required by a tool" (`durable/harness/harness_tools_test.go`) ports `harness-tools.test.ts:263` with Pi's inputs and checks that the first two events are both starts, in either order, and that the next model request carries the tool results `c1` then `c2`; it fails when the round runs sequentially or the results are reordered.

Remove when: never, unless Pi specifies the start order of the calls of a parallel round.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:8cf74b65f86e8cd2

## D180 A provider transport failure records its Go cause as a provider_transport_failure diagnostic

What: when a provider request fails in transport (reset, refused, DNS, dial or TLS timeout, TLS certificate failure, dropped response stream), PiG appends one `provider_transport_failure` diagnostic to the failed assistant message's `diagnostics`. Pi emits that diagnostic type only for the Codex WebSocket path and nothing for the openai, anthropic and google SDK failures. The diagnostic has Pi's codex shape: `{type, timestamp, error: {name, message, code}, details: {eventsEmitted, phase}}`, with `phase` `before_message_stream_start` or `after_message_stream_start`. `error.message` is the Go cause, `error.code` a Node-style code (ECONNRESET, ECONNREFUSED, EPIPE, ETIMEDOUT, ENOTFOUND, EAI_AGAIN, UND_ERR_HEADERS_TIMEOUT, UND_ERR_SOCKET, CERT_* and TLS codes) when one applies. The codex-only `configuredTransport`, `fallbackTransport` and `requestBytes` details are not written. The `errorMessage`, stop reason, retry classification and event order are byte-identical to Pi. Caller aborts record nothing.

Why: a reset, a dial timeout and a DNS failure all read `Connection error.`, so a log cannot tell them apart (#87).

Scope: `assistantStreamBuilder.fail` and `failUnfinished` (`ai/stream_builder.go`), classification in `ai/transport_diagnostic.go`. Purely additive on new failures. Stored entries are never rewritten or reinterpreted; an entry without the diagnostic loads and saves unchanged, and no migration exists.

Owner decision: owner, 2026-10-14 (in session): option A, non-invasive.

Call-site markers: `ai/transport_diagnostic.go` (`transportFailureDiagnostic`).

Evidence: `TestClassifyTransportCause`, `TestProviderTransportResetRecordsDiagnosticAndKeepsMessage`, `TestProviderTransportMidStreamRecordsDiagnostic`, `TestTransportDiagnosticLeavesEventStreamUnchanged`, `TestOldAssistantMessageWithoutDiagnosticRoundTrips` in `ai/transport_diagnostic_test.go`.

Parity allowance: none.

Remove when: Pi records a transport diagnostic for SDK request failures.

SCRUTINIZED:approved text-sha256:a11e50e6138dbc33

## D181 The commits of an unawaited durable job are ordered by goroutine scheduling, not by Pi's microtask hops

What: a job (a conversation-owned task) that its creator does not wait for commits its start, phase and terminal records in an order Pi fixes and PiG's reference harness (`durable/harness`) does not. In Pi one thread settles the job's commits against the generation chain's by microtask hops, the same every run: with the interop fixture's `note` tool leaving its `app.job` unawaited, Pi commits the tool call's settle, the job's first phase, the generation's tools outcome and the job's terminal commit as seq 13, 14, 15, 16 and the answer as 21 (300 of 300 fresh Pi runs of each variant and scenario, loaded or not, wrote one identical store). PiG orders the same commits by which goroutine reaches the Session line first, so now and then they land in another order: (A) the job's phase commits land before the tool call's settle commit; (B) the job's terminal commit lands after the generation's next turn, after the answer commit; (C) the job's phase and terminal commits both land after the generation's tools outcome; (D) a commit of the job merges into a neighbouring commit (one commit fewer, same writes). The effect on stored data is commit sequence numbers only: the content of every record is the same, and the only columns that differ from Pi's store are `entries.commit_seq` and `document_revisions.seq` (and `next_seq` after a merge). Every settled public read is unchanged: task receipts, `GetTask`, `ScanTasks`, `Inspect`, `WaitForIdle`, entries, `Context`, every document including `pi.inbox`, as-of reads and the `WatchEvents` sequence. One read taken at a moment differs: in shapes B and C the job's terminal commit is still pending when the answer commits, so a read taken as `Submission.Wait` returns can show the job live (`Inspect` lists it, `GetTask` shows it `running`), where Pi's order has it terminal. A model that takes a moment to answer makes B and C rare, and they need a busy machine: B and C were 38, 6 and 3 of 19,980 runs with the faux model delayed by 0 ms, 1 ms and 10 ms on an oversubscribed 14-CPU Mac (load average 50 to 100 with ten busy loops added), and 0 of 60,000 on a pinned 64-CPU socket of a 128-CPU Linux host (load average 29 to 35). Shape A, which has no transient, was 39, 54 and 63 on the Mac and 5, 11 and 12 on the Linux host.

Why: Pi's spec orders work by waiting, not by commit sequence. "There are no free-standing task dependencies: ordering comes from a task waiting on other tasks" (`spec.md` section 5.3, line 2086); "The scheduler serially reserves eligible tasks, then runs handlers off the Session line" (5.4, line 2092), so an invocation's commits race the generation's and nothing in the spec relates them; "Ordering is judged on committed records: code a child runs after its terminal commit is not ordered" (line 2384); and `Seq` "strictly increases between commits; gaps are permitted" (line 100; the Storage contract says the same at line 5172). A job nothing waits for is neither owned by the generation nor held by it, so its commits are ordered against the generation's by implementation, and in Pi the implementation is the microtask hops of its event loop. PiG's invocations run on their own goroutines and the reference harness has no model of those hops, so the order of independent chains follows the Go scheduler. Ordering them as Pi does would need a virtual microtask queue with per-await tick accounting in the harness, or the core engine (`durable/core`), whose `Settle` hook and hop model reproduce Pi's order but are not the default engine.

Scope: the commit order, and a read taken before the job's terminal commit, of a conversation-owned task that nothing waits for, against its conversation's generation commits, in `durable/harness`. Unchanged: the final state of every record, every settled read, the `WatchEvents` sequence, a job its creator awaits (the shipped interop fixture: 0 of 2,000 runs differ from Pi in each cell measured, see Evidence) and an ownership tree (a task waits for its owned work, spec 5.5). The Harness API returns no commit sequence number; they show in the store's `commit_seq` and revision `seq` columns and to a custom `Storage`.

Workaround: await the job (`WaitForTask`, or the tool waits for it as the interop fixture does) or `WaitForIdle` before reading its state.

Owner decision: 2026-10-18, owner in session: approve D181 with the transient read disclosed; ship 0.5.0 with the residual numbered; the exact fix goes through the core engine in 0.5.x.

Call-site markers: `durable/harness/scheduler.go` (`TaskScheduler.start`, where an invocation gets its own goroutine) and `durable/harness/generation.go` (`finishToolRound`, the generation's read of the round's outcomes). The point of use is also stated on `Harness.WaitForTask` in `durable/harness/types.go`.

Evidence: `TestUnawaitedJobCommittedAfterTheAnswerChangesOnlyOrder` (`durable/interop/unawaited_job_test.go`) holds the job's terminal commit back with a channel until the first turn has settled, so the order of shapes B and C is forced without a sleep. It checks that `Inspect` and `GetTask` show the job live when `Submission.Wait` returns, and that the same session with the job awaited shows nothing live; that every settled read above is equal between the two; that the raw tables equal Pi Durable's store for the same unawaited script once `commit_seq`, `seq` and `next_seq` are dropped, and that every difference with Pi's store is one of those columns; and that the Harness API view equals Pi's. It fails when a settled read, any other column or the transient differs; it does not fail when the engine orders the job as Pi does, because the hold fixes the order in either runtime. Sweeps, 2026-10-18, reference harness at `d6c21fca4b`, unawaited `none` and `after` variants of interop scenarios 1 and 2, no injected job delay, 36 cells of N=2,000 (N=1,000 under `-race`) at GOMAXPROCS 2 and 8 and GOMAXPROCS 2 under `-race`, with the faux model delayed 0, 1 and 10 ms (20,000 runs per delay): on the Linux host (one 64-CPU socket of a 128-CPU host, pinned, four busy loops, load average 29 to 35) mismatches against Pi's store were 5, 11 and 12, all shape A, none B, C or D; on the Mac (14 CPUs, ten busy loops, load average 50 to 100; each delay 19,980 runs) 79, 63 and 68, of which shapes B and C 38, 6 and 3, shape A 39, 54 and 63, shape D 2, 3 and 2. In every mismatching run (28 on Linux, 210 on the Mac) the settled reads, the `WatchEvents` sequence included, equalled those of a run in Pi's order. A further 6,000 (`after`) and 4,000 (`none`) Mac runs of scenario 1 at GOMAXPROCS 2 under `-race` found 5 more natural shape D runs (12 in all), with identical settled reads; the same hunt on the Linux host, 6,000 (`after`) and 4,000 (`none`) runs, found no mismatch. Awaited job (the shipped fixture): 0 of 2,000 per cell for scenario 2 at GOMAXPROCS 2 with and without `-race` and for scenario 1 at GOMAXPROCS 1, 2 and 8. Pi: 300 of 300 identical stores per variant and scenario, and 300 of 300 under load.
