## D230 `pig config` lists declared Package members that are missing from disk, marked `missing`

What: when a Package declares a member (an extension, skill, prompt or theme entry in its manifest) whose file is absent, `pig config` lists it in the resource list like any other row, followed by the warning-colored suffix `  missing`, and the toggle keys disable or enable it. Pi 1.5.2's config list is built from resolved paths that exist (`config-selector.ts` `buildGroups` over `ResolvedPaths`; `ResourceList.render` draws `${cursor}    ${checkbox} ${name}${suffix}` with no health marker), so a declared member that matches no file has no row there. The row is `ResourceItem.Health == "missing"`, set where the Package's members are resolved (`internal/packagemanager/resources.go`) and drawn after the name and the project-scope suffix in `ResourceList.render`. Every other row, the search, the navigation, the toggles and the header are unchanged and equal to Pi's.

Why: Pi skips a declared member that matches nothing, so startup proceeds, and gives the user no way to see or disable the stale entry in `pi config`. PiG keeps the entry visible so the user can disable it, which rewrites the Package filter exactly as for an existing member (`-extensions/missing-extension`).

Pi bug: none; this is an addition to Pi's behaviour for a state Pi hides.

Scope: the `pig config` selector (global and project scope) for Packages with declared members that are absent. Packages whose members all exist render exactly as Pi's.

Owner decision: 2026-11-03, the owner approved keeping the missing-member rows.
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:8426216ea6fd5167

Remove when: Pi lists declared-but-missing Package members in `pi config`, or the owner withdraws the feature.

Call-site markers: `tui/config_selector.go` (the `Health` suffix in `configResourceList.Render`), `internal/packagemanager/resources.go` (the `Health: "missing"` member).

Evidence: `TestConfigSelectorDisablesEnabledMissingPackageMember` (`coding/cli/config_selector_command_test.go`: the selector shows the enabled missing extension and Space writes `-extensions/missing-extension`), the `ConfigSelector` case of `TestSelectorDialogListComponentsNeverExceedRenderWidth` (`tui/component_width_table_test.go`: a `missing` row at every width).

Parity allowance: Pi has no row for this state, so no Pi-paired scenario can show it. Every other row of the list is compared with Pi byte for byte, escapes included, by `TestConfigSelectorContractMatchesPi` (`tui/config_selector_contract_test.go`, no probe holds a missing member; the dark, light and faint-dim system themes) and by scenario `selectors/18-config-project-toggle` (`escaped_output_equal`; only the D2 configuration-directory names, the allocated snapshot path and PiG's built-in `pig-login` row are normalized). Scenario `selectors/02-config-resources-list` compares the global list with the escapes stripped.

## D235 A session-graph client's runs share one ctx.signal when its SDK sends no run token, and when two runs were aborted before the host read them

What: in a Host whose run signal comes from a session-graph client (an ExtensionRunner an extension constructed), Pi's `agent.signal` is a new AbortSignal for every run (`agent.ts:345-347`: one `AbortController` per `activeRun`, made at `agent.ts:518-527`), so a handler that holds run 1's `ctx.signal` never sees run 2's abort, and sees run 1's own abort even when run 2 began before anyone read it. The wire is the answer of the client's `getSignal` (an opaque callback result the host reads leniently; no frame changes): `null` while no run is active, else `{"aborted": bool, "run": <positive integer>, "endedAborted": true}`.
- `run` is minted by the client's SDK, in the wrapper of the bound `getSignal` function, from one counter for the whole client process (extensions packed in one process never share a token). A token is new when the function returns another signal object than the wrapper's last answer, and the same object keeps its token; a `null` answer touches nothing. The wrapper holds the last signal object.
- `endedAborted` is present (and omitted when false) with a new token when the signal object of the previous answer had aborted by then, and is repeated on every answer of that token, so a lost read cannot lose it.
- The host (`graphSignal.read`) makes a new context for a token other than the held one, cancelling the held one first when `endedAborted` is set.
Where PiG still differs from Pi, and D235 applies: (1) a client that sends no `run`, or zero (a `run` is positive, so the host reads an explicit zero as no token, not as a malformed answer; an SDK built before this change, or a client that answers the wire by hand, sends none) keeps the old rule: two live runs with no read of "no run" between them are one context, and a handler that holds run 1's `ctx.signal` sees run 2's abort. The mixes degrade to today's behaviour: a new SDK with an old host (the old host ignores the members), an old SDK with a new host (no token). (2) Two runs that are both aborted when the host first reads them, with no live read between them, are one context: `ctx.signal === held` is true where Pi's two aborted signals differ (pinned by `TestGraphTwoRunsAbortedAtBirthShareOneContext`).

Why: (1) the host cannot tell runs apart without an identity from the client; an SDK before the token sends none. (2) A client whose `getSignal` returns a new signal object on every call (a derived signal) has a new token on every read. Once its run is aborted, a context made for every aborted read would be cancelled at birth, and the watcher (`context.AfterFunc` fires at once for a cancelled context) would sync, read, renumber the run and read again without end (the busy loop fixed in 892827ff8c). The storm guard bounds it: a read makes at most one context, and a token other than the held one makes none while the held context is cancelled and the answer is aborted, so an abort costs at most 4 reads of the client's `getSignal` (the sync that sees it, the watcher of the context it made, the watcher of the context it replaced, one more sync) and each request one read; case (2) is the price of that bound.

Per SDK (the porting rules: the same interface and the same behaviour in every SDK; only the implementation differs). Interface: the wire members above. Behaviour: the token and `endedAborted` rules above, the last signal held, a `null` answer touching nothing, one counter per client process. Implementation: Go `signalRuns` in `extensions/sdk/extension_runner.go` (a mutex per bound function; identity is `==` guarded by `reflect.Type.Comparable`, so a signal whose type cannot be compared is a new run on every answer; counter `atomic.Uint64`; the token is boxed once per run, not once per answer), Python `_signal_wire` in `extensions/sdk-py/pig_sdk/extension_runner.py` (a lock per wrapper and one for the `itertools.count`; identity is `is`), Rust `SignalRuns` in `extensions/sdk-rs/src/extension_runner.rs` (a `Mutex` per bound function; identity is `ProviderSignal` equality, which is `Arc::ptr_eq`; counter `AtomicU64`), Lisp `runner-signal-wire` in `extensions/sdk-lisp/src/extension-runner.lisp` (an `sb-thread` mutex per bound function; identity is `eq`; counter by `sb-ext:atomic-incf` on a global). Node: none; Pi's own `ExtensionRunner` runs in the Node process over real AbortControllers and there is no wire.

Pi bug: none. The difference comes from the wire and from the bound that keeps a derived signal from looping.

Scope: a session-graph client's ExtensionRunner only (a client of the host, not an extension's own `ctx.signal`); case (1) for clients without a token, case (2) for two runs aborted at birth with no live read between them. A Host over a Session reads the agent's own context, one per run.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy for 0.7.1 (SCOPE-PROPOSAL row M4, record only; the wire fix is a later row). The text of this record was rewritten when that wire fix landed (the run token, the storm guard, the per-SDK statement); the owner has not approved the rewritten text.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: no supported client sends an answer without `run` (the host may then require it and case (1) is gone), and the host can read an aborted run without renumbering it on every sync, so that the storm guard can be dropped and two runs aborted at birth are two contexts (case (2) is gone).

Call-site markers: `graphSignal.read` (`coding/session_graph_extension_runner.go`), `actions.GetSignal` in `contextActions` (`coding/session_graph_extension_runner_bind.go`).

Evidence: conformance rows `TestSessionGraphRunSignalAcrossSDKs` and `TestPackedSessionGraphRunSignalAcrossSDKs` (`test/extension-conformance/extension_runner_signal_test.go`; every SDK cell, isolated, shared and packed, against the report of Pi's own `ExtensionRunner` over real AbortControllers in the Node cell; the Go, Python, Rust and Lisp client scripts drop their own reference to the aborted run's signal before the next run is read, and force a collection where the language has one (Go `runtime.GC`, Python `gc.collect`, Lisp `sb-ext:gc`; Rust frees at the last drop), so a wrapper that does not hold the signal fails its cell; the Node cell is Pi's own runner, which holds nothing of the signals) and `TestSessionGraphRowsUnderAggressiveCollector`; host tests `TestGraphGetSignalTokenedRuns`, `TestGraphGetSignalEndedAbortedCancelsTheOldRun`, `TestGraphGetSignalErrorBetweenTokenedRuns`, `TestGraphGetSignalMalformedRunIsAReadError`, `TestGraphGetSignalZeroRunIsNoToken`, `TestGraphGetSignalAppliesTokenedAnswersInReadOrder`, `TestGraphSignalAnswerWithRunDecodesInAReleasedHost`, `TestGraphUnstableSignalIsReadOncePerRequest` (counts the client's `getSignal` calls: the storm bound), `TestGraphTwoRunsAbortedAtBirthShareOneContext`, `TestGraphSignalReadOfAHeldRunAllocatesNothing`, `TestGraphGetSignalIsOneObjectForARunAndItsAbort`; SDK unit tests `TestSignalRuns*` (Go, with `TestSignalRunsBoxTheTokenOncePerRun`), `tests/test_signal_runs.py` (Python), `signal_runs_tests` in `extension_runner.rs` (Rust) and `signal-wire-*` in `tests/graph-tests.lisp` (Lisp).

Pre-existing: yes (an earlier variant made a new context per read). Found in review on 2026-11-04.

## D266 An image block with an empty `mimeType` is named `image/unknown` in tool cards, where Pi names it with the empty string

What: `getTextOutput` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/tools/render-utils.ts`) draws a fallback line for each image block the terminal does not show, naming it `img.mimeType ?? "image/unknown"`. A block with no `mimeType` prints `[Image: [image/unknown]]` in Pi and PiG alike. A block whose `mimeType` is the empty string prints `[Image: []]` in Pi and `[Image: [image/unknown]]` in PiG. `ai.ImageContent.MimeType` is a plain string, so PiG treats an empty type as an absent one. The same call in Pi guards the image dimensions with a truthiness test (`img.data && img.mimeType`), which already treats `""` as no type. The difference shows in every card that draws `getTextOutput`: the bash, read, write, grep, find, ls, MCP and codemode result cards.

Why: PiG's output names the image with a type a reader can recognise instead of an empty bracket pair, and keeping absent and empty alike avoids a presence flag on a core public type (`ai.ImageContent`) for one fallback label. Only an extension or MCP server that sends `mimeType: ""` can reach it.

Pi bug: Pi 1.5.2's `??` fallback and its own truthiness guard disagree about `""`; not reported upstream.

Observable effect: Pi's tool card names an image block whose `mimeType` is the empty string `[Image: []]`, an empty bracket pair, where PiG names it `[Image: [image/unknown]]`.

Scope: `tools.GetTextOutput` and `codemodeTextOutput`; image blocks with an empty `mimeType` only. Blocks with a type, or with none, render as in Pi.

Owner decision: 2026-11-06, owner approved keeping PiG's output and recording this as a numbered divergence (D266).
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:2478db21b1bb7ae4

Remove when: Pi treats an empty `mimeType` like an absent one in `getTextOutput`.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/tools/render_text_output.go` (`GetTextOutput`), `internal/codingagent/tool_codemode_renderer.go` (`codemodeTextOutput`).

Evidence: `TestEmptyMimeTypeImageDiffersFromPi` (`internal/codingagent/empty_mimetype_image_divergence_test.go`), `TestCodemodeTextOutputNamesAnImageWithoutMimeType`, `TestGetTextOutput...` cases in `internal/codingagent/render_utils_pi_test.go`.

Parity allowance: `TestEmptyMimeTypeImageDiffersFromPi` runs the pinned Pi for a codemode card and a bash card with an empty `mimeType` and states both sides (`[Image: []]` in Pi, `[Image: [image/unknown]]` in PiG), with a control of a real type compared byte for byte. It fails when either side changes.

## D267 The plugins' `npm install` ends with its command, not with the processes that hold the command's output pipes

What: `InstallDependencies` (`chord/nextnode/plugins`, the port of `installDependencies` in `packages/chord/src/node/plugins/install.ts`) waits for the install command to exit, and for the end of its stdout and stderr only for five seconds after that (the stop grace, `exec.Cmd.WaitDelay`). A process the command started that inherited its pipes (a daemon npm left behind) no longer holds the install: it returns with the command's result, the output written so far, and the pipes' Go ends closed (what the child writes later is dropped and its writes fail with a broken pipe). A command that exited with code 0 is installed even when the grace expired. Pi settles the install on the child's `close` event, which fires only when the command's stdio streams have closed, so with a process left holding them Pi's install waits for as long as that process lives.

Why: A plugin install that hangs on a descendant the user cannot see blocks the plugin load behind it with no error and no way out but killing the app; the 0.7.1 fix of the Windows Node-host exit (commit 47ec6e80d8) found the same exposure in the Node host's own process wait and fixed it there (`NodeHost` waits for the process's exit, as Pi's workers do with `child.once("exit")`, `node/workers/process.ts:79`, so that path matches Pi and is no divergence). The install is the one place where PiG waits as Pi's `close` does, so it follows the exit instead. This is a cure of a Pi defect under the owner's rule of 2026-11-13T22:52:04Z ("fix all bugs"), not a difference forced by Go.

Pi bug: Pi's `installDependencies` hangs until a process that inherited npm's pipes ends (`child.on("close", resolve)`, `install.ts:217`). Not reported upstream.

Scope: `InstallDependencies`, and every other command the plugins' installer runs (the five-second rule sits in the shared runner `run`, so `git init`, `git fetch`, `git checkout`, `npm pack` and `tar` of `installPlugin` return at their command's exit under it too), in the case a process other than the command holds the command's stdout or stderr after the command exited. `WaitDelay` also counts from an abort: when the context ends, the command is sent SIGTERM as before, and a command that ignores it is now killed five seconds later and the install returns the abort's reason then (measured 6.0 s, against 30.1 s on 989927eff4, where `Run` waited for the command to end by itself). Pi rejects at once and sends SIGTERM and does not kill, so the abort path is closer to Pi, not further. Nothing differs when the command's streams close with it (every case Pi's tests cover): arguments, working directory, output text on failure, the abort path and the exit-code messages are Pi's.

Owner decision: 2026-11-05, approved through the coordinator for 0.7.1.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: Pi stops waiting on inherited pipes (its install settles on the command's `exit`, or bounds the wait for `close`). Then the call-site marker and this record go.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_install.go` (`InstallDependencies`, and `run` with the `WaitDelay` and the `ErrWaitDelay` case).

Evidence: `TestInstallDependenciesEndsWithTheCommandThoughAProcessItStartedHoldsItsPipes` (`chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_install_test.go`; red on 989927eff4: the install did not return in 25 s for exit 0 and for exit 1).

Parity allowance: none needed; Pi's install tests (`install.test.ts`, ported in `plugins_install_test.go`) pass unchanged, and none of them leaves a process holding the pipes.

## D268 The extension SDKs read tool arguments of any depth the host can send; Pi's runtime fails at a depth set by V8's stack

What: Pi's own runtime (the Node SDK) returns a tool's arguments only while V8 has stack for `JSON.stringify`. On Node 24.22.2 with the default stack, arguments shaped `{"zeta":1,"meta":{"d":[...]}}` come back as an object at d=4300 and fail with `RangeError: Maximum call stack size exceeded` at d=4400 (`JSON.stringify` fails past 4459 at the default stack and past 9103 with `--stack-size=2000`; plain `JSON.parse` reads any depth). On Node 26.12.1 (V8 14.6) a plain `JSON.stringify` of nested arrays or objects has no such limit (it returns at 2,000,000 levels), so Pi's runtime returns the object at every depth the host sends; `JSON.stringify` with a replacer function or an indent still runs out of stack there (at about 2700 and 5700 levels), and so does a plain one when the value holds a string V8 has not flattened (the concatenation `text += delta` leaves one, in Pi's provider streams and in the Node runtime's `append` operation of an event stream). The limit is V8 stack exhaustion: it moves with the call depth, the Node version, the platform and `--stack-size`, so no fixed Pi depth exists to reproduce. PiG's Go, Python, Rust and Lisp SDKs return the object, and the texts Pi writes for it, for every frame the host can send. An argument value can nest 10000 levels (the host's marshal validates it alone against 10000: d=9998 in the row shape above). None of the four ends its extension or its connection on deeper input: a request is answered with the RangeError message, a host call result is an error, a notification is dropped with a line on stderr. The Node SDK is Pi's own runtime; PiG's Node runtime copies each event-stream payload for its handler (iteratively), and a handler receives the object as in Pi, up to where V8's own `JSON.stringify` runs out of stack (about 4400 levels on Node 24; on Node 26 only where the payload holds an unflattened string).

| SDK | What reads the frame | Bound |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Go | `json.UnmarshalFrame` (`extensions/sdk/json`; `Unmarshal` and the host's marshal keep the standard 10000) | 10064 levels of the whole frame |
| Python | `json.loads`, then a non-recursive parser (`_loads_deep`); ordering and stringify are iterative | 10100 levels (`MAX_FRAME_DEPTH`); `json.loads` alone stops at about 9997 on 3.12 and about 990 on 3.11 |
| Rust | `from_json_str` on a 64 MiB decode stack (the arguments of a request or notification and the operations of an event-stream delta); a request with such a value runs on a 64 MiB thread, the connection's main loop on an 8 MiB one | 10016 levels (`MAX_NESTING_DEPTH`) |
| Lisp | the JSON reader and writer recurse to 256 levels and keep deeper containers on a stack of their own; ordering, event-stream copies, `JSON.stringify` of conversation values, the bigint check and palette comparison are iterative (the image keeps SBCL's default 2 MB control stack) | 10016 levels (`*max-json-depth*`) |
| Node | Pi's own `JSON.parse` and `JSON.stringify` | V8's stack (about 4400 levels on Node 24; a plain `JSON.stringify` has none on Node 26) |

Why: Before, an extension could die on arguments Pi's own host sends it. The Go SDK ended its connection from d=9997: its reader validated the whole frame against encoding/json's 10000-level limit, while the host validates a request's arguments alone, so a frame the host sends nests a few levels past it (a limit of the codec, not a stack). The Python SDK failed the call from about 1000 levels, and from about 990 on Python 3.11, because its ordering and `JSON.stringify` were recursive and its JSON reader is bound by the recursion limit. The Rust and Lisp readers ended the connection past 4096 and 2000 levels. Rust's `Context::tool_params` returned none past 128 levels. Rust's event-stream deltas (a `message_update` whose tool call carries deep arguments, a `tool_execution_update`) ended the connection past 128 levels. Matching Pi's observable result for the arguments Pi accepts, and failing a call (never the extension) beyond what an SDK reads, keeps tool calls with legitimately nested JSON working. Pi's exact cut-off is not reproducible.

Pi bug: none; Pi's limit is the stack of the JavaScript engine, not a defect to report.

Scope: arguments, results and events (the whole payload of an event and the operations of a `message_update` or `tool_execution_update` delta) read by the Go, Python, Rust and Lisp SDKs, in every placement (isolated and packed), up to the host's 10000 levels. Not the Node SDK. Not a value nested past 10000 levels (the host refuses to send one).

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:a6de9d33566f33d3

Remove when: Pi parses and returns tool arguments without a stack-bound depth limit, or the owner decides the SDKs should stop at Pi's observed depth. Then the call-site markers and this record go.

Call-site markers: `extensions/sdk/protocol.go` (`readLoop`), `extensions/sdk-py/pig_sdk/__init__.py` (`Extension._read`), `extensions/sdk-rs/src/protocol.rs` (`MAX_NESTING_DEPTH`), `extensions/sdk-lisp/src/json.lisp` (`*max-json-depth*`; Lisp is not scanned by `check-divergence-consistency.sh`).

Evidence: `TestToolParamsNestingAcrossSDKs` (`test/extension-conformance/tool_params_deep_test.go`; red on 03c551f3e2 for Go at d=9998, and for Python, Rust and Lisp), `TestModelStreamEventWithDeepToolArgumentsReachesEverySDK` (`model_stream_deep_event_test.go`), `TestDeepEventStreamDeltasReachEverySDK` (`deep_event_delta_test.go`; deep `message_update` and `tool_execution_update` deltas, red for Rust on c1da695c1d), both over isolated and packed placements, and each SDK's own suite for the frames past its bound (`extensions/sdk/protocol_deep_frame_test.go`, `extensions/sdk-py/tests/test_deep_frames.py`, `extensions/sdk-rs/src/surrogate_tests.rs`, `extensions/sdk-lisp/tests/json-tests.lisp`).

Parity allowance: the row pins every SDK's outcome at depths 200, 1000, 4000, 5000, 9000 and 9998: the object for the Go, Python, Rust and Lisp SDKs at all six; for the Node SDK (Pi's own runtime) the outcome of the running Node's own `JSON.stringify` of the same value, which the row measures with the `node` the extensions run on rather than assuming a depth: on the qualified Node 24.22.2 the object at 200, 1000 and 4000 and V8's RangeError at 5000, 9000 and 9998, on Node 26.12.1 the object at all six. The event rows (`TestDeepEventStreamDeltasReachEverySDK`) pin the payload of a `message_update` or `tool_execution_update` delta, in arrays and in objects, at 200, 3000, 4000, 9000 (and 1 after them): every SDK hands its handler the payload, except that the Node SDK's handler throws V8's RangeError from its own `JSON.stringify` of an event of 9000 levels that carries the deep value, as a handler does in Pi, which gives the handler the object without copying. The row asserts which events: where the running Node's plain `JSON.stringify` overflows at that depth (Node 24) every such event, and for a tool update that holds `meta` the fixture's own handler as well; where it does not (Node 26) only the events of a stream whose replica holds a string an `append` operation made since it was last set, which the row reads from the frames the host wrote, never from the runtime's code. The row also replays those frames through the runtime's own event-stream code in the same `node` and requires the handlers to fail on exactly those events and to report the exact payload on the others, so a runtime that leaves unflattened strings where Pi's handler would not fails the row. Each depth is followed by a call to the same extension. Pi's runtime gives the object at d=4300 and the RangeError at d=4400 on Node 24; the row does not pin those, because the cut-off moves with the Node version.

## D269 A system fd or rg is accepted only if `--version` names it, and the `@` autocomplete runs fd in a new session

What: Pi's tools-manager `commandExists` runs `spawnSync(cmd, ["--version"], { stdio: "pipe" })` and counts any result but a spawn error as "installed", with no timeout, in Pi's own session. `getToolPath("fd")` therefore returns the first of `fd`, `fdfind` that spawns, and Pi runs it at startup (`ensureTool("fd")`) and on `@`. Debian's `fdclone` package installs a file manager as `/usr/bin/fd`; it opens `/dev/tty`, so Pi's `@` autocomplete, `find` and startup hand it the terminal. PiG's base only looked the name up (it never spawned), so it started FDclone at `@` and in `find`. PiG now:

| Step | Pi | PiG |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Candidate names | `fd`, then `fdfind` (rg: `rg`) | same |
| Acceptance of a PATH candidate | `spawnSync(cmd, ["--version"])` returns without a spawn error | `<bin> --version` exits 0 and stdout starts with `fd ` or `fdfind ` (rg: `ripgrep `); Debian's sharkdp build prints `fdfind 10.3.0` |
| On mismatch | the impostor wins | next name, then the managed download in `<agentDir>/bin` |
| The probe | Pi's session, no timeout | started like the searches (`nodespawn.SetProgram`), in a new session on unix or a console of its own without a window on Windows (`nodespawn.HideWindow`), stdin `/dev/null`, stdout a pipe; a 5 s bound (1 s pipe grace) that kills the process group on unix (the process on Windows); a completed verdict is cached per path, mtime and size, a timeout or start failure is not |
| `@` autocomplete fd search | Pi's session | new session on unix (a cancel kills the group); on Windows a console of its own without a window. `DETACHED_PROCESS` is not used: a Scoop or Chocolatey shim started under it opens a visible console window for the real program |
| `find` and `grep` fd/rg search | Pi's session | unchanged: the caller's session and group, so a terminal Ctrl+C in print mode reaches the search with the agent |

The managed copy in `<agentDir>/bin` is PiG's own download and is not probed.

Why: typing `@` over ssh on a host with fdclone launched a full-screen file manager, and a real fd installed as `fdfind` was never reached. Pi has the same bug.

Pi bug: `commandExists` in `packages/coding-agent/src/utils/tools-manager.ts` accepts any program that spawns, so an unrelated `fd` passes. To report upstream.

Scope: `tools.LookupToolPath` and `ToolsManager.GetToolPath` (so `EnsureTool`, `BeginEnsureTool` at startup through `ensureManagedTools`, the tools status line, and the `find`, `grep` and `@` lookups), and the `@` autocomplete's fd run. A system `fd` or `rg` whose `--version` line is not one of the prefixes (a wrapper, a custom build) is no longer used; it falls through to the managed download, or to nothing offline. Narrower than the first proposal: `find` and `grep` do not run their search in a new session, because the identity probe already keeps an impostor out and a new session would orphan rg and fd on a terminal Ctrl+C in print and json modes.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: Pi checks that the program it found is the tool, not only that it spawns. Then the probe, the autocomplete's new session and this record go.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/tools/tool_probe.go` (`systemToolIdentity`, the 5 s probe bound, the 1 s pipe grace), `tui/file_autocomplete.go` (`walkDirectoryWithFd`).

Evidence: `TestLookupToolPathSkipsImpostorFdForFdfind`, `TestLookupToolPathRejectsLoneImpostor`, `TestLookupToolPathChecksRipgrepIdentity`, `TestLookupToolPathAcceptsFdWrapperWithoutShebang`, `TestToolProbeCachesPerFileRevision`, `TestProbeToolVersionTimesOutHungProgramAndItsChildren`, `TestProbeToolVersionDoesNotCacheStartFailure`, `TestVersionProbeCannotReachTheTerminal`, `TestFindAndGrepKeepSearchInCallersSession` (`internal/codingagent/tools/tool_probe_unix_test.go`, `tool_probe_linux_test.go`) and `TestWalkDirectoryWithFdRunsInANewSession`, `TestWalkDirectoryWithFdCancelKillsTheGroup` (`tui/file_autocomplete_session_linux_test.go`). The impostor and no-shebang cases fail on a45b578455, where a fake `fd` ahead of `fdfind` was returned.

Parity allowance: `TestLookupToolPathFindsToolsAsPiDoes` keeps comparing lookup order with Pi's `getToolPath` on Windows with fixtures that answer `--version` as the tool, so only the identity check differs.

## D270 After a /tree choice PiG holds its frames until the navigation returns; Pi paints the old transcript first

What: Pi's tree handler (interactive-mode.ts:5751-5865) awaits `session.navigateTree` (5840) and then runs `chatContainer.clear(); renderInitialMessages()` (5857-5858). The key that closes the "Summarize branch?" prompt asks for an immediate frame (tui.ts:1158-1161) that runs on `process.nextTick` (tui.ts:1044-1058), before the microtask that resumes the await. So Pi paints the old transcript with the prompt gone first, and the fork point replaces it on the next frame: a few milliseconds without an extension handler, and the whole await of a `session_before_tree` handler that awaits. PiG's navigation runs on a worker and decodes the session's records for the context: on a 27 MB, 600-entry session of 88 KB tool results that is about 60 ms at 16 cores and about 190 ms at 4 (about 750 ms at 1) after the parallel read splits by record bytes (it was 830 ms at 16 before), and the owner saw the old transcript for about a second before the fork point. PiG now holds its frames from the choice that starts the navigation (No summary, Summarize, the custom prompt's submit, or the tree selection when `branchSummary.skipPrompt` is set) until the navigation has returned and the transcript is swapped, so the prompt stays on screen and the first frame after the choice is the fork point. The transcript's entries are read on the worker, so the repaint decodes nothing on the owner loop.

Why: the owner asked that the user never see the old leaf after selecting. Pi's old-transcript frame is brief enough not to be seen; PiG's decode is not always brief, and a hold is the only way to show no stale frame whatever the navigation takes.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: `/tree` navigation. The hold starts only from the choice of the prompt's own dialog. It ends when the navigation returns, when an extension opens a dialog or an overlay, when a summary shows its status, and when the run is aborted before navigating. Esc and the selector's choice "Summarize with custom prompt" are not held (the hold starts at the submit of the custom instructions editor, and an Esc there returns to the selector). While it lasts, nothing that only requests a frame is painted: the user's typing, `setStatus`, `setWidget`, footer and header changes. A paint that names a render (`showStatus`, `showError`, notifications) still paints the old transcript with the prompt gone. The hold has no upper bound: a handler that awaits for a minute keeps the "Summarize branch?" prompt on screen for a minute, where Pi shows the old transcript.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: PiG's navigation completes within one frame for every session, or the owner decides to match Pi's brief old-transcript frame.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/interactive_tree.go` (`navigateTree`), `internal/codingagent/interactive.go` (`holdFrames`), `internal/codingagent/slash_session_handlers.go` (`treeNavigateWithSummarize`), `internal/codingagent/ext_ui_context.go` (`runDialog`).

Evidence: `TestTreeNavigationPaintsNoStaleFrameBeforeTheTranscriptSwap` (red without the hold: the editor painted during the navigation), `TestTreeSummaryPromptChoiceHoldsTheFramesOfItsClose`, `TestTreeDialogsOfOtherContextsNeverStartTheHold`, `TestTreeNavigationPaintsAnExtensionDialog` and `TestTreeNavigationPaintsAnExtensionOverlay` (`internal/codingagent/interactive_tree_frames_test.go`); scenario `tree/15-tree-navigation-no-stale-frame` (fails 42 of 119 sampled panes with `holdFrames` a no-op).

Parity allowance: scenario `tree/15-tree-navigation-no-stale-frame` runs a `session_before_tree` handler that awaits 600 ms. Both programs end on the same pane (`output_equal`). PiG's pane, sampled every 10 ms from the choice until "Navigated to selected point" shows, never shows the old leaf with the prompt gone (`pig_no_stale_frames`); Pi's samples are not asserted, because Pi shows that pane throughout the await.

## D272 The upgrade type check runs in a driver program that the go command builds from the staged SDK, not in the pig binary

What: `pig extension upgrade`, the startup SDK-drift offer and `extensionsAutoUpgrade` type check a Go extension with `go/types`. In Pi there is no SDK to drift from and no upgrade path, so no behavior of Pi is changed. In PiG the `pig` binary no longer links the type checker (`go/types`, `go/importer`, `go/printer`, `go/format` and the gc and gccgo importers: 511 KB of `go/types` symbols and about 1.4 MB of file). The type checker is the program `extensions/sdk/upgrade/driver`, a part of the staged Go SDK. `pig` runs `go run -trimpath ./upgrade/driver` in the staged SDK directory, with the go command and the environment that already list and build the extension. It sends the `LoadOptions` as JSON on standard input and reads the plan as JSON from standard output. The plan, the report, the diff, the exit codes and the error text are the same as before.

What a user can see:
- The analysis runs only when a Go source extension fails to build with an SDK drift error (the startup offer, `extension upgrade`, `extensionsAutoUpgrade`). Startup with no failing Go extension is not affected.
- Warm cache: +0.06 s to +0.2 s per analysis (`pig extension upgrade --dry-run`: 0.63 to 0.67 s before, 0.71 to 0.84 s after), and about +0.09 s per start that has a Go extension that fails to build (0.14 to 0.18 s before, 0.24 to 0.27 s after).
- The first analysis after a Go update, an SDK update or with an empty Go build cache compiles the driver: +4 s to +7 s (measured 4.2 to 4.7 s at start, 7.2 to 7.7 s for `extension upgrade`, 5.0 s on top of an empty build cache; go 1.27.3, linux/amd64, a loaded 16-CPU host, so ±10 %). The Go build cache keeps the driver, so later analyses do not pay it.
- It works offline: the SDK module has no requirement, so the driver build needs no download. The measurements ran with `GOPROXY=off GOTOOLCHAIN=local`.
- `pig` stages the Go SDK before the analysis (`EnsureSyncedLang`). A config root whose staged SDK cannot be written fails the analysis with the staging error. Before, the analysis ran from any SDK root the build found. A stock `pig` stages the SDK at startup already.
- A cancelled analysis closes the driver's standard input, which cancels the driver's `go list`.
- With no `go` on the path the text is the same as before. A Piglet Binary that compiled out the Go SDK cannot analyze a Go extension (D92).

Why: the type checker was 1.4 MB of a binary every user downloads, and only the upgrade path runs it. The go command is already required by that path, because it lists and builds the extension. The driver is compiled by the user's own toolchain, so the compiler export data and `go/types` match.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: the go command that the toolchain resolver picks, on every platform. The `pig` binary links no symbol of `go/types`, and `go/types`, `go/importer` and the gc and gccgo importers are not among the dependencies of `cmd/pig`: `coding/cli` imports `extensions/sdk/upgrade/plan`, the names of the upgrade that read no types (the rules, the classification of a failed build, the plan and the driver's request and reply), and not `extensions/sdk/upgrade`, which imports `go/types`. Before that, the import of `extensions/sdk/upgrade` kept 106,773 bytes of `go/types` symbols in the linux_amd64 binary through package initializers. `extensions/sdk/upgrade` keeps every exported name, as aliases of the same declarations, so the API of the SDK module does not change. The process tests of the driver run on Linux, macOS and Windows: the fake go command is the test binary itself (8c67993ab1). Only the CI jobs of macOS and Windows prove the same code on those systems. One `pig extension upgrade --dry-run` on the D109 fixture with a fresh `PIG_HOME`, rebuild included, takes 2.80 s before the driver and 3.17 s after (+0.37 s, 10 warm runs on xd670); `pig` peaks at 37.0 MB against 41.1 MB, and the driver child at about 12.6 MB.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-06.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: the owner prefers the type checker in the binary again, or Go ships `go/types` in a form that the binary can link at no size cost.

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/extension_upgrade_analyze.go` (`analysisDriver`, `analysisKillDelay`, `analyzeExtension`, `runAnalysisDriver`), `extensions/sdk/upgrade/driver/main.go` (`main`).

Evidence: `coding/cli/extension_upgrade_driver_test.go` runs the real driver and the real go command for a fixture extension (the plan, the error text, cancel, a missing go command). `extensions/sdk/upgrade/driver/main_test.go` covers the request and reply round trip and the cancel by closing standard input. `strip_upgrade_link_test.go` requires that no build of `coding/cli` references a symbol of `extensions/sdk/upgrade`. `test/perfgate` `TestReleaseBinaryLinksOnlyAllowedPackages` reads the symbol table of the release binaries (linux_amd64, darwin_arm64, windows_amd64) and fails on a `go/types` symbol (the forbid table of `automation/perf/linked-packages.toml`); `TestPlanDoesNotImportTheTypeChecker` of `extensions/sdk/upgrade/plan` holds the same for the dependencies of the package `pig` imports. The upgrade tests of `extensions/sdk/upgrade` and `coding/cli` (`Upgrade`, `SDKDrift`, `AnalysisDriver`) pass with the driver in the path. Review `reviews/sizeclimb-sdk.md` measured the latencies above before and after on xd670.

## D274 A packed Go or Lisp member whose own code ends the shared process stops alone, and the members that shared the process restart and lose their in-memory state and in-flight calls

What: Pi loads every extension into one process, and an isolated PiG extension has a process of its own. A packed Go or Lisp cell shares one process among its members, and neither language can contain a failing thread of an extension's code in the process that serves the others: a goroutine one member started that panics, or a thread one member made that signals a condition nothing handles, ends the process. The SDKs recover what they can: a panic or condition in any callback of the extension's own code that an SDK runs on a goroutine or thread it started (a handler, a tool, a listener, an editor or overlay component, an MCP credential store's `WithRefreshLock`, a settings storage's `WithLock`, a compact or nested-call callback) is that call's error, in an isolated and in a packed process alike. What remains is code the extension itself started.

When such a thread ends the process, the host reads the trace the process wrote to its stderr log, only the trace of the panic or condition that ended it, and only when the process ended that way. A Go process that an unrecovered panic ended exits with status 2 and leaves the runtime's section: the last line of the log that begins `panic: `, its message and continuation lines, a blank line, and the header of the goroutine that was running (`goroutine N [running]:`), with nothing written between them and no `fatal error: ` line after it. The blocks the SDK writes for the panics it recovered (a handler's, a listener's) are handled failures and decide nothing. The frames of that goroutine, and its `created by` line, name a member when a frame's package is the member's import path, or belongs to the module of exactly one member. A Lisp backtrace (the last `Backtrace for:` section) counts only when SBCL's own `unhandled condition in --disable-debugger mode, quitting` line follows it, and names a member by the package that qualifies the function of a frame; the arguments SBCL prints after the function, which include the condition's text, name nobody. A trace that names exactly one member blames it. One that names two, or none, blames no one, and so does a process that ended any other way than by that panic or condition: `os.Exit`, a runtime fatal error (which ends with status 2 as a panic does, and writes `fatal error: `), `sb-ext:exit`, a kill. Whatever a member logged before decides nothing then: the text a member writes for a panic it handled in the form the Go runtime writes its own (`panic: ` and `debug.Stack()`), or a backtrace it prints (`sb-debug:print-backtrace` writes SBCL's `Backtrace for:` header), is not what ended the process. Four limits remain, and each blames or spares a member wrongly only in the case named: a Go member that logs such a block and then ends the process itself with `os.Exit(2)` is read as a panic of the member whose frames the block holds; and a Lisp thread of one member that calls another member's function in tail position names that other member, because SBCL's tail-call merging drops the calling frame from the backtrace (Go's `created by` line always names the starter, so Go is not affected). The other two need a trace crafted by the members' own code, written while the process is dying: a bare `debug.Stack()` block (it has no `panic: ` line) that a member writes between the blank line that follows the runtime's panic message and the runtime's goroutine header is read as the running goroutine's, and a backtrace a Lisp sibling prints between the culprit's backtrace and SBCL's quit line is read as the one that ended the process. The host then, when it blames one member,
- stops the old process and every member's connection, and quarantines no cell;
- stops the blamed member and reports it, with the cause the process wrote (`panic: ...`) and the log that holds it, as stopped until `/reload`, which is what an isolated extension's crash does; a `/reload` runs it isolated, in a process of its own, and never again beside the members it took down; and
- starts the other members again together in a new shared process, at once, and reports each of them as restarting.

The restarted members run their factories again, so an extension's in-memory state starts over, and a call that was in flight when the process ended fails with the connection error and is not replayed. An isolated extension's crash ends that extension only; here a sibling's crash restarts a member that did nothing wrong. A trace that names no member, or two (a kill, `os.Exit`, a runtime fatal error, a package two members share), stops every member of the cell until `/reload` restarts them, as before. A Rust thread that panics or a Python thread that raises ends that thread, not the process, so neither language reaches this path; a Node cell has its own recovery (D20).

Why: Before, one member's goroutine ended every sibling and told them to run `/reload`, though the trace says whose code it was. Stopping only the member whose code failed matches the isolation an isolated extension gives, and the one thing packing cannot give an in-process language, a boundary around a failing thread, is replaced by a restart of the others. Recovering inside the thread is not possible: a goroutine or thread that the extension started has no frame of the SDK to recover in.

Pi bug: none; Pi runs every extension in one process and has no process boundary to restart.

Scope: the Go and Lisp SDKs' packed cells (a placement of the same factory the isolated cell runs), when the process ends with a trace that names exactly one member of a cell of two or more. Not an isolated extension, not a Rust or Python cell, not a Node cell, not a process that ended any other way than by an unrecovered panic or an unhandled condition (`os.Exit`, a runtime fatal error, a signal).

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-07 (proposed by the 0.7.1 coordinator).

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: an extension's goroutine or thread can be contained in the process that serves other extensions, or the owner decides that a crash of a packed member stops its siblings until `/reload`. Then the call-site marker, the trace readers and this record go.

Call-site markers: `coding/extension/host/subprocess/packed_native_recovery.go` (`recoverNativeProcess`).

Evidence: `TestPackedProcessCrashBlamesTheMemberWhoseGoroutineEndedIt` (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/packed_native_recovery_host_test.go`: a Go cell of two; the other member's handler had panicked and been recovered earlier; one member's goroutine panics; the other restarts, the culprit stops and a reload runs it isolated, no cell is quarantined, and only the culprit is reported stopped), `TestPackedProcessExitStopsTheWholeCell` (the same file: `os.Exit` after a recovered panic, and `os.Exit`, a fatal error and a kill after a logged panic block, blame no one and stop the whole cell), `TestPackedLispProcessEndedWithoutAnUnhandledConditionStopsTheWholeCell` (`test/extension-conformance/packed_crash_blame_test.go`: a printed backtrace, then `sb-ext:exit` or a kill), `TestGoTraceCulpritReadsOnlyWhatEndedTheProcess` and `TestLispTraceCulpritReadsOnlyWhatEndedTheProcess`, `TestPackedCrashBlameAcrossSDKs` (`test/extension-conformance/packed_crash_blame_test.go`; Go and Lisp, isolated and packed), `TestGoTraceCulpritNamesTheMemberWhoseCodeEndedTheProcess` and `TestLispTraceCulpritNamesTheMemberWhosePackageQualifiesAFrame` (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/packed_native_recovery_test.go`: recovered blocks, `os.Exit`, fatal errors, a condition's text and two named members), and for the callbacks the SDKs recover `extensions/sdk/callback_guard_test.go`, `TestEditorComponentFailingKeyIsReportedAndFinished` (`test/extension-conformance/editor_input_failure_test.go`) and `a-failing-compact-callback-does-not-end-the-process` (`extensions/sdk-lisp/tests/core-tests.lisp`).

Parity allowance: the row pins what a packed and an isolated placement share after one member's own thread ends its process: the blamed member is reported stopped with the log that holds its panic, the sibling answers a command and is never reported stopped, and a `/reload` brings both back. It does not pin that the sibling keeps its in-memory state, because it does not (the residue above).

## D277 A Node renderer component that handles the mouse itself takes the click only, and the host answers it handled for the whole component

What: Pi routes every mouse event of a fullscreen frame to the component under the pointer, and the answer of the component decides what the renderer does next (`tui-alt-screen.ts` `handleMouseEvent`, `handleSelectionMouseEvent`). A custom message or entry renderer of the Node SDK returns a component that lives in the extension's process, so the host cannot know its answer before it has to answer the terminal; it must not wait for the extension (AGENTS.md TUI rules). For a tree built from Pi's own `MouseRegion`, `Container` and `Box` the host needs no answer: the Node runtime finds the rectangles in which a `MouseRegion` answers by one walk over the tree that follows the three rules by which Pi's `dispatchMouseEvent` hands an event down (a `Container` and a `Box` to the child whose rows hold it, a `Box` inside its padding, a `MouseRegion` answering for every event that reaches it), sends them with the frame (`regions`), and the host hit-tests them. A click outside every region (the padding of a card) is left unhandled and falls through to text selection and copy-on-select; a click inside is handled and delivered to the component; Pi's compaction, branch-summary and skill cards and a `MouseRegion` card of an extension therefore match Pi byte for byte. The residue is a component whose tree has a handler that is not a `MouseRegion`, `Container` or `Box` (a hand-written `handleMouse`): PiG answers a click anywhere in it as handled at once and delivers it to the component (`dispatchMouseEvent`, coordinates local to it), and leaves every other event (press, release, drag, move, wheel) unhandled, so selection and scrolling work over it. A hand-written handler that takes press, drag, wheel, or a click only on some cells gets none of those events, and a click on a cell it would not have handled is handled and clears a text selection. A `MouseRegion` whose closure answers an event other than `click` (press, drag, wheel) gets only the click. A `MouseRegion` whose closure declines a left click (returns no result for it) is still answered handled, because the host cannot run the closure before it answers the terminal: the selection is cleared and copy-on-select is skipped, where Pi leaves the click unhandled. The SDKs whose renderers return a kit view are not affected: the host hit-tests a view's mouse regions from their description and answers exactly as the region says.

Why: asking the extension is impossible without waiting for it, and answering every event inside the component (D107 §4.3, the ui.custom rule) would make every clickable card swallow its text selection and the transcript's wheel scrolling.

Pi bug: none.

Observable effect: only an extension that writes its own `handleMouse`, a `MouseRegion` that answers press, drag or wheel, or a `MouseRegion` whose closure declines a click differs from Pi, in the cases above.

Scope: `render_message` and `render_entry` components of the Node runtime whose tree has a hand-written mouse handler, and the non-click events of a `MouseRegion` closure. Not tool renderers, widgets, header and footer (they take none yet, a recorded gap), not kit views, not trees of `MouseRegion`, `Container` and `Box` clicked.

Owner decision: approved by owner Michael Kinsy on 2026-11-07, in this narrowed form only.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: the host can ask the extension synchronously for a component's answer, or Pi's own components of a separate process need none.

Call-site markers: `coding/extension/host/subprocess/render_proxy.go` (`HandleMouse`), `coding/extension/host/subprocess/runtime-node/runtime.mjs` (`renderTranscriptComponent`).

Evidence: `TestRenderProxyNodeComponentTakesClickOnly` (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/render_proxy_mouse_test.go`) pins the residue; `TestRenderProxyNodeMouseRegionsAreHitTestedLikePi` and `TestRendererClickOutsideTheRegionFallsThrough` pin that a click outside the regions is unhandled and one inside is handled and counted; the tmux scenario `extensions-runtime/96-message-click-expansion` compares the clicks on the regions and on a card's padding with Pi's, but it cannot tell the narrowed behavior from the wide one (a padding click that the host wrongly handled still reaches Pi's own dispatch inside Node, which leaves it unhandled, so the screen is the same: only selection and copy-on-select differ, and a capture does not show them); the conformance row `TestRendererClickOutsideTheRegionFallsThrough` is the guard. `TestNodeMouseRegionsAreTheCellsPiDispatchReaches` (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/node_mouse_regions_test.go`) pins the walk against Pi's own `dispatchMouseEvent` asked about every cell, over 600 generated trees of `Container`, `Box` with padding and `MouseRegion`, over layouts rendered at another width, and over a layout that changed after the render, and checks that no handler of the extension runs and a 4000-line card costs milliseconds (the per-cell probe took 1.8 s at width 100 and 2.9 s at width 200).

Parity allowance: none beyond the scenario above.

## D430 Go callers start a call and await it apart (Reference.Start, PendingCall) and run a handler's blocking remainder from outside the host package (AwaitingCtx, Resume)

What: Pi's `ref.method(args)` starts the call at once and returns a Promise. A caller may start several calls before awaiting any (Files v2's pipelined `append`s, which the call window allows) and the host accepts them in the order they were started. A blocking Go `Reference.Call` cannot express that, so `Reference.Start(method, args, ctx)` is the synchronous half (the call is admitted and its handler's synchronous part has run when it returns) and `PendingCall.Wait(ctx)` the awaiting half. `Start` on a name that is not a method returns `<name>: <method> is not a method` (`host_pending.go:30`), where Pi's `ref.method()` throws a TypeError. `host.AwaitingCtx(ctx, work)` is `Awaiting` for a caller outside the package, which cannot name the host's loop: the blocking remainder of an `onLive` or `on` handler (the awaits of Pi's async function). `host.Resume(ctx, work)` is the other way for a Go caller to resume in the loop after a wait: it runs `work` as one synchronous section on the goroutine of a handler that suspended (a transaction, a blocking call), inline when the handler still runs in its admitting section, as the awaits of Pi's handler resume inside the loop.

Why: AGENTS.md "TypeScript async/Promise parity": an awaited Promise is a blocking call, but a Promise started and awaited later is two Go operations. Without Start the pipelined-append behaviour could not be tested or used through a reference; without AwaitingCtx a package outside `chord/next/host` (the app kit) could not declare an `onLive` routine.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: additive Go API of `chord/next/host`; no Pi behaviour changes.

Owner decision: owner, 2026-11-13T19:27:20Z (`handoffs/0.8.0/OWNER-DECISIONS.md`, "procee[d]" on the six recommendations): "D430 APPROVED: Go callers start a chord call and await it apart (Reference.Start, PendingCall; AwaitingCtx, Resume); additive, no Pi behaviour change."
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:e393a4a4c3a7aabe

Remove when: never; it is the Go form of an un-awaited Promise and of a handler's async remainder.

Call-site markers: `chord/next/host/host_pending.go` (`AwaitingCtx`, `Resume`), `chord/nextnode/app/files.go` (`FilesFacet`, which uses `AwaitingCtx`), `chord/nextnode/app/files_support_test.go`.

Evidence: `TestChunkedUploadsWriteAppendsSentWithoutWaitingAtTheirOwnOffsetsByteForByte` and `TestChunkedUploadsFinishAnUploadOnlyOnceTheAppendsSentBeforeItLanded` (`chord/nextnode/app/files_test.go`), `TestChunkedUploadsDropTheUploadsAPreviousRunLeft` (the `onLive` routine), `TestResumeRunsAfterAHandlerSuspendedInASectionOfTheLoop` (`chord/next/host/host_resume_test.go`).

Parity allowance: none.

## D460 The app kit's facets are Go core facets beside the plugins' Node host

What: Pi loads the kit's facets and the plugins into one host. PiG's plugins run in a Node subprocess (`docs/plan/chord-plugins-node-evaluator.md`), so `StartServer`/`StartWorker` load the kit's facets (info, sign-ins, owner, pairing, files, themes, translations, telemetry ingest, the HTTP facet) as Go core facets on the server's Go host (a relay without an address on a worker); the plugins' `NodeHost` is a child link (`Accept` on the server, `WorkerPlugins.Load` on a worker). The local types plugins use (`AppInfo`, `SignIns`, `WorkerInfo`) reach the Go objects through `plugins.BridgedFacet`. `HostData` must be served at the worker's address, which the NodeHost serves, so it is a JavaScript core facet (`host_data.mjs`, written per start with the server's principal and a secret token) forwarding `exportTo` to the bridged Go local `chord.app.data.export`; plugins can see the bridged type but cannot call it without the token (in Pi they cannot reach the facet at all). Translations and themes are served to the plugins by Pi's own facets in the plugins' Node host: `translationsFacet` (server and every worker) and `themesFacet` (server only, as Pi's `worker.ts` loads none) are JavaScript core facets (`core_facets.go`: the Node host makes each by calling the vendored Pi package's function, `plugins.CoreFacet.Export`), loaded before the plugins, so a plugin's contribution to `Translations` or `Themes` and a plugin object's `self.registry(...)` meet in one registry as in Pi. What stays different: the Go host's `chord.translations` and `chord.themes` (for the app's own Go facets) keep a registry of their own, so a Go facet's contribution is read by Go readers of the Go host and not by a plugin, and a plugin's is read by a plugin and not by a Go facet's `Host.Registry(Translations)`; no facet of the kit contributes to either. `chord.http` and `chord.http.authenticators` stay Go-host registries (a plugin cannot reach them). So a Go facet of the app (`options.Facets`) that contributes translations or themes is read only by Go readers of the server's Go host: neither server or worker plugins nor pages read it. A page or terminal reads the registry of its own host (the client's `translationsFacet`), which the plugins of its kind (web, tui) contribute to: contribute what a page must show from a web or tui plugin.

Why: the plugin runtime is Node; the facets are Go because the kit is a Go port. Moving them to the Node host would port the kit to JS; serving HostData from Go needs the Go relay to answer for its child's address, which a chord host does not do. Translations and themes are Pi's own vendored JavaScript facets: serving them to the plugins costs no port.

Scope: `chord/nextnode/app`, `chord/nextnode/plugins` (bridge).

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08, OWNER-DECISIONS #19.

Remove when: the plugins' host and the kit's facets run in one process (the plugin runtime is not a subprocess), or a chord host can answer for its child's address so `HostData` needs no JavaScript module.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/app/app.go` (`start`), `chord/nextnode/app/worker.go` (`runWorker`), `chord/nextnode/app/data.go` (`hostDataModuleText`), `chord/nextnode/app/core_facets.go` (`piCoreFacets`).

Evidence: `app_test.go`, `app_workers_test.go`, `app_export_test.go`, `registries_plugins_test.go`.

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

## D462 The server and each worker have two stores, not one

What: Pi's host has one store, one commit queue and, for the server's data of an address, one SQLite commit (`packages/chord/src/store/host-data.ts:316-336` `importHostData`, `:341-354` `deleteHostData`, both through `commitOperation` `:359-374`; `packages/chord/src/node/app/app.ts:865` and `:891` call them). PiG has two hosts per server or worker (the Go host, the plugins' NodeHost), and a store has one writer (a singleton meta row: `last_seq`, `next_id`), so each has its own: the kit's (`kit.sqlite` in a worker's directory, `store.sqlite` on a server) and the plugins' (`store.sqlite` in a worker's directory, `plugins.sqlite` on a server). Nothing commits across them. Three things still differ from Pi because of that. (1) The data directory holds both files. (2) `importData` and `deleteData` commit twice: the NodeHost's store, then the Go host's (the NodeHost runs Pi's `exportHostData`, `importHostData` and `deleteHostData` for its side through three control requests of its process, `plugins_node_host_data.go`; the Go host's side is the owners it serves, `Host.Serves`). A failure the process survives is undone as Pi's flow undoes it: a definite failure before either commit, or of the NodeHost's, puts the old directory back; a definite failure of the Go host's commit after the NodeHost's put its part undoes the NodeHost's (a refused import's part is deleted, with `existing: "replace"` the old part is put back), so the end state is the one Pi's undo leaves; if that restore fails too, nothing is undone (the new directory in place, the old one aside) and the rejection carries both errors; an unknown outcome (`unavailable/storage_failed`) of either commit undoes nothing and says so with Pi's text. A kill is covered by an intent, which Pi, with one commit, has no need of: a file in `server/imports/` (`<kind>.<key>.json`, with the address's old data in both hosts beside it as `.node` and `.go`) written and flushed (file, then its directory) before the first commit and removed, with its directory flushed, right after the second. At start, after the plugins' load and before the server serves anything (`resolveIntents`), a leftover intent is resolved with the code of the live undo: an import is UNDONE (both stores restored from the saved parts, the staging or new directory removed, the old directory back from where it was set aside), a delete is COMPLETED (the NodeHost's rows, the Go host's, the directory: Pi leaves the repetition of a delete to its caller too, `app.ts:889-890`). An import is undone and never completed because a kill cannot say which commit ran, and an import that refuses an address that has data would read its own first commit as "has data". Every step of the resolution is safe to repeat, so a kill during it is finished by the next start. (3) A running worker's export (`PackRunning`) takes the kit's commit queue (its export's first part), then has SQLite copy the plugins' file at one instant (`VACUUM INTO` over a read-only connection, a read transaction the NodeHost's writes do not disturb) and exports the copy, so the two stores' export is one point; the kit's writes wait for its archive, the plugins' writes proceed meanwhile and the archive does not contain them (the store keeps them). Pi holds its one store's commit queue (`store.export`, `archive.ts:3-4`). The copy costs the plugins' store's size in temporary space (`.chord-export-*`, beside the archive).

No longer a difference: the archive. PiG's data archive is Pi's one format in both directions. The export writes one `store.archive` (the plugins' records and the kit's tables together, merged at one point) and one `server.archive` (both hosts' records), the entries Pi's `unpackEntries` accepts (`archive.ts:178-222`: the manifest, `store.archive`, `server.archive`, `files/`); `kit.archive` and `kit-server.archive` are gone, with their readers. Pi's own unpack and its host-data import accept an archive PiG made, and PiG imports an archive Pi made, with the kit's Files tables routed by owner into the kit's store (so the Files index arrives) and the server part's records to the host that serves their owner. The sentence that the owner decides whether to build a converter is gone: it was built, decided under the owner's rule of 2026-11-13T22:52:04Z ("fix all bugs") and decision 29 of 2026-11-17.

Observable effect: an operator finds two SQLite files per host directory and, after the first import or delete, a `server/imports/` directory that is empty between operations. After a kill in the middle of an import or a delete the next start reports one app error of kind `interrupted_data_change` per interrupted operation: "undone" (the address's directory, plugins' records and server rows are as they were before the import) or "completed" (a delete: all gone). That kind is PiG's own member of `AppErrorKind` (`chord/nextnode/app/app.go`); Pi's `AppError` at the pin has `host`, `http`, `workers`, `plugins`, `base_build` and `server_scan` (`app.ts:115-121`). A consumer written for Pi's kinds gets it in its `OnError` like any other and reads `Kind` and `Error`: one that logs every report generically prints it (the importing child of the kill tests does, `fmt.Fprintln(os.Stderr, err.Kind, err.Error)`; `TestAStartReportsTheInterruptedImportItUndid` shows the report arrive), one that switches on Pi's six kinds with no default ignores it. A kill before the intent is durable leaves the address as it was (a staging directory `.chord-import-*` may stay: nothing refers to it). A kill between the commits, or after the second and before the intent is removed, undoes the whole import: Pi's single commit would have kept that import; PiG undoes it, because the caller never received success. An import whose intent cannot be removed after the second commit is reported as an error ("the import is done but its record cannot be removed") and the next start undoes it. An intent that cannot be used (it does not parse, has another shape, names another address, a saved part is missing or does not restore, a store fails again) is never guessed at: its address does not start and its import, delete and export are refused with an error that names the file, the other addresses serve, the start reports it, and the intent stays for the next start; an operator fixes the cause or removes the three files. That is how the app treats one unusable address today, not a new policy: a worker whose start fails fails that address's worker with the public error `worker failed to start` until a retry or a rollout, and the server and the other addresses run on (Pi `packages/chord/src/node/workers/workers.ts:454-470` and `:499`, `START_FAILED` `:251`; PiG `chord/nextnode/workers/workers_workers.go:894-972`). With store files that hold garbage in one worker directory, a start serves the other address, a hold of that address fails with `worker failed` when the ready timeout (an app option, `AppOptions.Timeouts.ReadyTimeout`) ends, and the app reports a `workers` error (`file is not a database`) and a `host` error (`resolve_failed`): `TestOneAddressWhoseStoreFilesAreGarbageFailsItsOwnWorkerAndTheOthersServe` (about 13 seconds, the timeout being set to 10 by the test). A blocked address fails its hold the same way (`TestAnIntentThatCannotBeUsedBlocksItsAddressOnly`). The window between the plugins' load and the end of the resolution allows nothing to read or write an address's data: a plugin's facet setup only declares the types it serves (it has no documents, routines or natives, `packages/chord/src/host/facet.ts:1-4`), so no plugin object is live and no plugin code touches a store, the HTTP component answers 503 until the load is reported done and the delegation to the NodeHost's link waits for it, and a worker starts only when a request holds its address, which cannot come before; `TestNothingIsServedAndNoWorkerStartsUntilTheStartHasResolvedItsIntents` probes the port at every step of the undo (every answer 503) and records the first worker start after "resolved". The archive of an address is importable by Pi and by PiG whichever made it. An export of a running worker needs the plugins' store size in free space beside the archive, and the archive does not contain the plugins' writes made during it.

Scope: only apps built with the kit (`chord/nextnode/app`): `ImportData`, `DeleteData` and `ExportData` of an address, and the start's resolution of what a kill left. The refused import, the definitely failed one, the unknown outcome and the refused delete keep Pi's flow and Pi's messages; the intent adds only the kill, and a request of an address with no intent behaves as before. The archive format is Pi's, not a divergence. Pi's server, with one store, never needs any of this.

Why: the node-evaluator design (`docs/plan/chord-plugins-node-evaluator.md` §store): the Go store is an in-process engine and a store is not shared over the link; sharing one sqlite file between two engines would corrupt the id allocator. Nothing commits across two SQLite stores, and neither can hold a record that the other's commit clears, so the intent is a file beside both.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08, OWNER-DECISIONS #19, for the two stores; the one archive format and the intent that makes a kill all or nothing by the owner's rule of 2026-11-13T22:52:04Z and decision 29 of 2026-11-17.

Remove when: (1) the files: the plugins run in the server's (or worker's) own host process with one store, or one store file can be shared by two hosts. (2) the two commits and the intent: the same condition, or Pi imports and deletes an address's server data in more than one commit and adds the same guard. (3) the export at one point across two stores and its temporary space: the same condition.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/app/app.go` (`openServer`, `start`, the worker kind's `Start`, `workersFor`, `ExportData`, `ImportData`, `placeAndCommit`, `DeleteData`), `chord/nextnode/app/import_intent.go` (`resolveIntents`), `chord/nextnode/app/worker.go` (`runWorker`), `chord/nextnode/app/archive.go` (`PackRunning`, `UnpackStaging`), `chord/nextnode/plugins/plugins_node_host_data.go` (`NodeHost.ExportHostData`, `ImportHostData`, `DeleteHostData`).

Evidence: the kill (a child process of the test binary stopped at a seam and killed by its pid): `app_import_kill_test.go` `TestAKillBetweenTheTwoCommitsOfAnImportLeavesTheAddressAllOrNothing`, `TestAKillBetweenTheTwoCommitsOfAReplaceImportPutsTheOldDataBack`, `TestAKillBetweenTheTwoCommitsOfADeleteFinishesTheDeleteAtTheNextStart`, `TestAKillAfterTheSecondCommitBeforeTheIntentIsRemovedUndoesTheFinishedImport`, `TestAKillDuringTheUndoAtStartIsFinishedByTheNextStart`, `TestAStartReportsTheInterruptedImportItUndid`, `TestAnIntentThatCannotBeUsedBlocksItsAddressOnly`, `TestOneAddressWhoseStoreFilesAreGarbageFailsItsOwnWorkerAndTheOthersServe`, `TestAnImportIsRefusedWhenItsIntentCannotBeWritten`, `TestTheIntentStandsFromBeforeTheFirstCommitUntilTheOperationEnds`, `TestTheIntentIsFlushedBeforeTheFirstCommitAndItsRemovalAfterTheSecond`, `TestNothingIsServedAndNoWorkerStartsUntilTheStartHasResolvedItsIntents`; the live path, `app_export_host_test.go` (the two-part host data: a round trip, the undo of the NodeHost's commit, both errors when its rollback fails, the unknown outcome, the delete of both); the archive format, both directions, with Pi's own code run by a Node script (`testdata/pi_archive_oracle.mjs`): `archive_pi_interop_test.go` `TestImportsAPiMadeArchiveWithItsFilesIndex`, `archive_pi_export_test.go` `TestPisOwnUnpackStagingAcceptsAPiGArchive`, `TestPisOwnImportOfHostDataAcceptsAPiGArchivesServerPart`, `TestAPiGArchiveRoundTripsThroughPiGWithEverythingTheStoresHold`, `TestAPiMadeArchiveRoundTripsThroughPiGBackIntoPi`, `TestAPiGArchiveRoundTripsThroughPiBackIntoPiG`, `archive_pi_files_tables_test.go` `TestGoFilesTablesAreIdenticalToPis`, `archive_split_test.go`, `archive_merge_test.go`; the export, `data_export_test.go` `TestPackRunningExportsBothStoresAtOnePointWhileTheyAreWritten` (both stores written during 8 exports: each archive holds exactly the rows its manifest counts), `app_export_test.go`.

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-18 text-sha256:c802940b9a8e8a1f, first approved 2026-11-08, amended 2026-11-10 for Pi 1.5.2 host data, amended 2026-11-17 for the final text (two stores, the two commits with the intent, one export point) by principle (owner decision 29), extends OWNER-DECISIONS #19

## D464 FormatNumber and FormatTime take closed option types and return errors (P1-3)

What: Pi passes `Intl.NumberFormatOptions` / `Intl.DateTimeFormatOptions` through to `Intl`. Go has no ICU, so the output comes from tables generated by running the pinned Node (v24.22.2, ICU 78.3, CLDR 48) over every locale ICU has data for (`automation/gen/generate-intl-tables.mjs`, `make intl-tables`, part of `pin-bump`; `chord/next/surfaces/intl_gen.go`). The tables cover a closed set: `NumberOptions` (minimum/maximum fraction digits, 0 to 100) and `TimeOptions` (`timeStyle` short|medium, or any of hour/minute/second numeric|2-digit, with optional `hour12`): what Pi's call sites pass (none, `{timeStyle: "short"}`, `{hour: "numeric"}`) and their siblings. Anything else (time zone, zone names, calendars, eras, fractional seconds, day periods, `hourCycle`, currency/percent/compact/unit, significant digits, rounding modes, Unicode locale extensions such as `-u-nu-arab`) cannot be expressed; a Unicode extension in the language's locale returns `ErrLocale`. Both functions return `(string, error)` for what Intl throws a RangeError/TypeError for (min > max fraction digits, an option out of range, an ill-formed tag, a style with fields) as `ErrOption` / `ErrLocale`; before, min > max was silently coerced. An empty `TimeOptions{}` is not an `Intl` error: `Intl.DateTimeFormat` with no options prints the date, and PiG returns `ErrOption` (`intl_time.go:123`), because a date is outside the closed set the owner approved; no Pi call site passes `{}`. The language is an explicit first parameter of both functions (`FormatNumber(lang *Language, ...)`, `FormatTime(lang *Language, ...)`). An unavailable or aliased locale resolves as Intl does (CLDR aliases, a measured exceptions table, BestAvailableLocale, then the platform default).

Why: a hand table or x/text would be a different function: x/text is CLDR 32 and half-even (312 differing outputs in the review's probe, 75 with no options), and has no date patterns. The generator derives symbols, affixes, grouping (including minimumGroupingDigits), numbering system digits, hour cycle, padding and day periods from `formatToParts` and refuses to emit unless its renderer reproduces `format()` byte for byte over a corpus (942 number locales x 10 option sets x 59 values; every time locale x every shape x 24 hours x 6 minute/second pairs).

Root data and aliases (RevChordIntl P3-3, P3-4): when neither a tag nor the platform's locale has data, ICU formats in its root locale. The generator observes it in child Nodes under `LANG=xx_<REGION>` and `LC_ALL=` (the process's default locale is read from the environment; on Windows it is the user's locale, as ICU reads it there): the numbers equal en-US's (checked over the same corpus), the times follow the region's hour cycle (221 regions have a row of their own, the rest and no region share another) and a variable set to the empty string is the locale `und` with its own row. Compound legacy aliases (`hy-arevmda` is `hyw`, `zh-hakka` is `hak`, `zh-xiang` is `hsn`) are probed from `Intl.getCanonicalLocales` over language x variant. Not modeled: root data for a platform tag that carries a script or variant with its own cycle, and `-u-` value aliases.

Scope: `chord/next/surfaces`.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08 (OWNER-DECISIONS #7, after the review's wording fixes).

Remove when: Go has an `Intl` with the pinned Node's CLDR, or a Pi call site passes an option outside the closed types (the types then grow and the generator with them).

Call-site markers: `chord/next/surfaces/surfaces_format.go` (`FormatNumber`), `chord/next/surfaces/intl_time.go` (`FormatTime`).

Evidence: `TestNumbersMatchNode`, `TestTimesMatchNode` (random corpus against a live Node, errors included; `INTL_DRIFT_CASES`, `INTL_DRIFT_SEED`), `TestFormatsNumbersLikeTheOracle`, `FuzzFormatNumber` (against an independent decimal reference), `FuzzFormatTime`, benchstat in the READY line.

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08

## D465 FormatTime takes a time.Time, not `Date`, `number` or `string`

What: Pi's `new Date(at)` accepts a `Date`, epoch milliseconds or an ISO/date string (`new Date(string)`'s parser, with its engine-defined formats). Go takes a `time.Time`; a caller converts milliseconds (`time.UnixMilli`) or parses its own string. The zone is `time.Local` (the process's TZ, as a `Date`'s default is), whatever location `at` carries. An invalid `Date` (a RangeError in Pi) cannot be expressed. Zone data: Go reads the system zoneinfo (or the embedded `time/tzdata`), Node uses ICU's bundled tz data with backzone history. Instants before 1970 in zones that tzdata links to another (Europe/Amsterdam before 1937: Go prints Brussels' LMT, Node Amsterdam's) and a host whose tzdata release differs from Node's bundled one can print different clock times (RevChordIntl P3-5: 0 mismatches in 19 other zones around every 2025-2027 transition). The evidence for this paragraph is a reviewer's probe, not a test in the repository.

Scope: `chord/next/surfaces`.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08 (OWNER-DECISIONS #7, after the review's wording fixes).

Remove when: never for the `time.Time` parameter (it is the Go form of a `Date`); the zone-data difference goes when Go's tzdata and the pinned Node's ICU zone data agree.

Call-site markers: `chord/next/surfaces/intl_time.go` (`FormatTime`).

Evidence: `TestFormatTimeShowsTheInstantInTheProcessZone`; `TestFormatTimeInAZoneWithADaylightSavingChangeIsTheClockNodePrintsUnderTheSameTZ` (`chord/next/surfaces/intl_zone_test.go`: the process zone across a daylight-saving change, compared with Node under the same `TZ`).

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08

## D466 No per-language formatter cache

What: Pi keeps a language's default `Intl.DateTimeFormat`/`NumberFormat` in a `WeakMap<Language, Formatters>` because a formatter costs far more than a format. Go formats from static tables with a binary search per call (no formatter object), so the "are collected with the language" case has nothing to test and is designed out. A canonical tag (what `Language.Locale` holds) allocates only the result string (one allocation per call); a non-canonical tag or the platform default locale allocates up to six times to canonicalize it (`EN-us` 5, `sh-RS` 3, `de-DE-1996` 2, the platform default under LANG=de_DE.UTF-8 6, measured by RevChordIntl P3-6).

Scope: `chord/next/surfaces`.

Pi bug: Not a Pi bug.

Owner decision: approved by the owner (Michael Kinsy) on 2026-11-08 (OWNER-DECISIONS #7, after the review's wording fixes).

Remove when: never: Go has no formatter object to cache; it goes if a format stops being a table lookup.

Call-site markers: `chord/next/surfaces/surfaces_format.go` (`FormatNumber`).

Evidence: `TestFormatsAllocateOnlyTheResultForCanonicalTags`.

Parity allowance: none needed beyond the evidence above.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-08

## D502 `TestPlugin` tests a plugin on the production topology: its host is the Node subprocess and the stubs are Go facets, not JavaScript values handed to one in-process host

What: Pi's `testPlugin(dir, { kind, modules, stubs, ... })` (`packages/chord/src/node/plugins/testing.ts`) builds the plugin, evaluates it in the test's own process with live JavaScript `modules`, loads it with live `stubs` (`PluginFacet[]`) on one new in-memory host and returns that host.
