What the parity evidence of writer mode is, then: the scenarios that compare a captured screen compare what tmux shows after the writer's bytes; scenarios 68 and 69 assert what the renderers wrote, not what reaches the terminal; one scenario, `fullscreen/19`, asserts what the terminal keeps of the writer's bytes beyond the screen it shows.

## D626 A model object an extension set is replaced by the registry's model only when the registry's entry for its provider and id changes

What: Pi's `_refreshCurrentModelFromRegistry` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:3501-3513`) replaces the Session's model with `getModel(provider, id)` unless that is absent or the same object (`refreshedModel === currentModel`), and it runs only after an extension's `registerProvider`, `registerNativeProvider`, `unregisterProvider`, `registerVirtualModel` or `unregisterVirtualModel` (`agent-session.ts:3626-3645`). So Pi distinguishes two objects an extension may pass to `pi.setModel`: a registry model edited in place is the registry's own object and keeps its edit until the registry composes that model again, while an edited copy (`{...model, contextWindow}`) or an object built by hand for an id the registry lists is replaced by the registry's model at the next of those five calls, made by any extension for any provider. PiG keeps every such object until the registry's entry for that provider and id changes, and then takes the registry's model: an edited copy survives another extension's provider or virtual-model (un)registration where Pi drops it. An object of an id the registry does not list is kept in both, and one whose id the registry starts to list is replaced in both.

PiG also refreshes on every committed registry change (`Session.refreshCurrentModelFromRegistry` observes `ModelRegistry.ObserveChanges`: a remote catalog refresh, a `models.json` reload, a credential-driven recomposition, as well as the five calls), where Pi's Session keeps its model object across the changes that are not one of the five calls. A request for a model the host built from a name also takes the registry's current model of that name (`ModelRuntime.prepareRequest`), where Pi's request sends the model it is given. Both predate this record and were not recorded: for a model taken from the registry they give the Session and its requests the registry's current entry, which Pi's Session only gets when the model is selected again. Both differ from Pi since v0.3.0 (the Session's observer of `ObserveChanges` and `prepareRequest`'s `runtime.GetModel` are in v0.3.0: `coding/session.go:561`, `coding/services.go:443`) and are kept, because following Pi would break released behaviour. A model object an extension set is sent as it is, as in Pi.

Why: the extension wire carries the model object as JSON, not its identity, so the host cannot tell an in-place edit from a copy. Replacing every object that differs from the registry's entry would make the upstream-tested shape (issue 9631: an edited registry model set from `before_agent_start`) lose its edit at PiG's own background catalog refreshes, which Pi does not have. Keeping the object until its entry changes is Pi's in-place case, and for a model set from the registry unchanged it is what PiG did before.

Pi bug: none.

Scope: `coding.Session.refreshCurrentModelFromRegistry`, `coding.ModelRuntime.BindModelObject` and `coding.ModelRuntime.prepareRequest`; a model object set through an SDK's `setModel` over the extension wire that differs from the registry's model of the same provider and id. A model the registry lists, set unchanged by its object or by its `provider/id` name, is not in scope: it follows the registry as before.

Owner decision: approved (owner, 2026-11-13T01:13:59Z: "Proceed with recommendations."; the line of that decision for this record: "D626 APPROVED (setModel object kept until its registry entry changes)."). Drafted by the SetModelImpl lane on 2026-11-12.

SCRUTINIZED:approved

Remove when: the extension wire can tell an in-place edit of a registry model from a copy, or Pi changes the rule of `_refreshCurrentModelFromRegistry`.

Call-site markers: `coding/model_catalog_backend.go` (`keepsModelObject`).

Evidence: `coding/session_model_object_refresh_test.go` (`TestRegistryChangeAndTheModelObjectOfTheSession`: a registry model as it is, a named model, an edited registry model, hand-built objects with a listed and an unlisted id, each across an unrelated registry change and a change of the model's own entry by an extension re-registration, a `models.json` edit and a remote catalog refresh).

Parity allowance: observable only to an extension that passes `setModel` an edited copy of a registry model, or a hand-built object with a listed id, while another registration of a provider or virtual model happens during the session.

## D628 auth.json is read once per availability pass, where Pi stats it on every credential read

What: Pi's `AuthStorage.readLatestData` stats the auth file on every credential read (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/auth-storage.ts:409`, `getFileRevision`). An availability refresh reads each provider's credential two or more times (`packages/ai/src/models.ts:729-738` `getAuthenticatedProviders`, `packages/coding-agent/src/core/model-runtime.ts:343-357` `runAvailabilityRefresh`); all of those reads run in one macrotask, so a write by another process lands before all of them or after all of them. The one writer that can land between them is an extension provider whose own auth check rewrites auth.json on disk during the synchronous part of that check: Pi's later reads of the same pass stat again and use the rewrite. PiG makes one revision check at the start of a pass and serves the whole pass from it, with or without extension providers (`ai.WithFileReadSnapshot`): the asynchronous availability refresh (`ModelRegistry.AvailabilityReadContext`) and the synchronous passes `ModelRegistry.GetAvailableModelData` and `ModelRegistry.AvailableProviderCount`. Such an on-disk rewrite is used by the next request and by the next pass, not by the rest of the pass it was made in.

Unchanged: a write through the credential API in this process (`Modify`, `Delete`, a login, a logout, a token refresh) ends the view at once, and the reads of the same pass that follow it use the write. A `!command` key is evaluated on every read, and a store that holds one is statted on every read. A pass whose extension providers do not rewrite the file publishes what Pi's pass publishes. A request, a login, a logout and a single provider's check are not passes and make their own revision check. A file that becomes malformed or unreadable during a pass is reported by the next request and the next pass with the text they report when it breaks between two passes.

Why: an interactive start made 455 revision stats (two synchronous passes of 126 to 131 each and one asynchronous pass of 176, in a fresh home with three models.json providers), 4 to 5 per provider; the built-in llama.cpp extension registers a provider in every start, so the earlier one-view rule, which excluded every pass with an extension provider, never applied to the start's refresh. With one check per pass the same start makes 19, one of them per pass.

Pi bug: none; Pi's stat per read is correct, only costlier.

Scope: `internal/codingagent` (`AvailabilityReadContext`, `GetAvailableModelData`, `AvailableProviderCount` and the auth helpers they call) and `ai` (`WithFileReadSnapshot`, `ReadRawCredential`). The models store's view (models-store.json) follows the same operation.

Owner decision: owner approved 2026-11-12T18:45Z: "one auth.json check per pass, with a test that a write mid-pass is used by the next request".
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:48913fccff6ed8fc

Remove when: Pi reads its auth storage once per availability refresh, or an extension API exists through which a provider's check announces that it changed stored credentials, so the view can end there. The credential API already ends the view for writes made through it in this process; no API covers a check that writes the file itself.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/model_availability.go` (`AvailabilityReadContext`).

Evidence: `coding/model_availability_auth_pass_test.go`: `TestExtensionCheckRewritingAuthFileIsUsedByNextRequest` (the divergent case: the pass publishes from the view of its start, the next request and the next pass use the rewrite), `TestAuthWriteDuringAvailabilityPassIsUsedByNextRequest` (another process writes during each kind of pass: an API key, an OAuth token refreshed elsewhere, an OpenAI-compatible provider with a custom base URL, a credential removed, a credential added; the next request succeeds or fails exactly as a process started over the new file), `TestCredentialAPIWriteDuringAvailabilityPassIsUsedByTheSamePass`, `TestAvailabilityPassStatsEveryReadAfterACommandKey`, `TestAvailabilityPassStatsEachAuthFileOnce` (at most one stat per auth file per pass), `TestBrokenAuthFileDuringAvailabilityPass`; `internal/codingagent/model_availability_read_context_test.go` (`TestAvailabilityReadContextGivesEveryPassOneAuthView`); `coding/model_availability_auth_view_test.go` (`TestAvailabilityPassAuthViewMatchesPiUpstream`: a pass without extension providers publishes what Pi publishes for a rewrite before, during and after it).

Parity allowance: no parity scenario can make an extension provider's check rewrite auth.json between two credential reads of one pass, so no scenario pins the difference; `TestExtensionCheckRewritingAuthFileIsUsedByNextRequest` is its regression test. Every scenario of the oauth, model-runtime-store-catalog and startup families compares as before.

## D629 A prompt that arrives while another prompt is being admitted in RPC mode is rejected with an error, where Pi answers it `started` and never runs it

What: in `--mode rpc`, two `prompt` commands without `streamingBehavior` that reach standard input in one chunk (written with one `write`) are handled by Pi before either of them has started a run. Pi 1.5.2's `prompt()` awaits `_preparePrompt` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/agent-session.ts:2191`, since #9154), so both prompts pass the `isStreaming` check in it (`:2227`) before either reaches `_startRun` (`:2195`). `_startRun` sets `_isAgentRunActive = true` (`:1944`) without checking whether a run is already starting, so both prompts answer `{"success":true,"data":{"disposition":"started"}}` (`:1960`). The second prompt's `agent.prompt(messages)` (`:1969`) then throws `Agent is already processing a prompt. ...` (`packages/agent/src/agent.ts:383-387`); its `finally` emits `agent_settled` with `aborted:false` (`:1986`, `_emitAgentSettled` `:1139-1146`), and RPC mode drops the error because the preflight already answered (`packages/coding-agent/src/modes/rpc/rpc-mode.ts:425-429`). The client reads, in this order: the first prompt's `started`, the second prompt's `started`, `agent_start`, `agent_settled`, then the first prompt's run, its `agent_end` and its `agent_settled`. The second prompt's message never appears, no run is made for it (`messageCount` is 3 afterwards) and no error names it.

That `agent_settled` also sets `_isAgentRunActive = false` (`:1141`) while the first run streams, so from then until the run ends Pi's `get_state` reports `isStreaming: false`, and a further `prompt` without `streamingBehavior` is answered `started` and dropped in the same way, with one more `agent_settled`.

PiG answers the second prompt `{"success":false,"error":"Agent is already processing. Specify streamingBehavior ('steer' or 'followUp') to queue the message."}`, the error both programs give a prompt that arrives while a run streams. The error is written before the first prompt's `started`; one `agent_start`, one `agent_end` and one `agent_settled` follow. `get_state` reports `isStreaming: true` while the run streams and a further `prompt` without `streamingBehavior` gets the same error.

Written one after the other (the second after the first prompt's `agent_start`; 50 ms apart in the probe) both programs agree: the first is `started` and the second is rejected with that error.

Pi main is the same: at `bacd45b9c` (150 commits after `v1.5.2`, read 2026-11-12) `prompt()` is `agent-session.ts:2175-2209`, the `isStreaming` check is `:2240`, `_startRun` is `:1956-2001` (`:1957`, `:1973`, `:1982`, `:1999`), and `rpc-mode.ts` and `agent.ts` do not differ from 1.5.2.

Why: a client is told that its prompt was not taken and can send it again or queue it. Pi accepts the prompt and loses it without an error, so a client that waits for the run of a prompt answered `started` waits for a run that never comes, and reads an `agent_settled` that belongs to no run it can see.

Pi bug: A prompt is answered `started` and never run; no error is emitted; an `agent_settled` is emitted for a run that never started, and it clears the busy state of the run that did. An upstream issue draft is the owner's to post; none has been posted.

Scope: RPC mode (`pig --mode rpc`): two `prompt` commands whose handlers overlap before either run starts. `rpcAdmission.promptInput` passes both, as Pi's `_preparePrompt` does; `rpcAdmission.startPrompt` (`coding/cli/rpc_admission.go`) calls `Session.BeginRunStart` (`coding/session_run_state.go`), which rejects the second because the first marked the Session busy. Prompts that name a `streamingBehavior`, `steer`, `follow_up` and extension commands are not affected. The other caller of `BeginRunStart`, `Session.prepareContentRun` (`coding/session.go`), checks `IsStreaming` itself first and was not compared with Pi for this record.

Owner decision: 2026-11-12T23:22:43Z owner: "proceed with recommendations": "RPC batch prompts (owner line 5): KEEP PiG's explicit rejection, recorded as a numbered divergence."
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:768e4c48eddc3ce2

Remove when: Pi rejects the second prompt of a batch with an error, or stops answering `started` for a prompt it does not run. `TestRPCPromptsWrittenInOneChunkDivergeFromPi` runs the pinned Pi and fails when Pi's side changes.

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/rpc_admission.go` (`startPrompt`), `coding/session_run_state.go` (`BeginRunStart`).

Evidence: `coding/cli/rpc_prompt_one_chunk_pi_test.go` (`TestRPCPromptsWrittenInOneChunkDivergeFromPi`: the pinned Pi and PiG on the same input, one chunk, one chunk followed by a `get_state` and a third prompt while the first runs, and one after the other, where both agree) and `coding/cli/rpc_prompt_order_test.go` (`TestRPCPromptsOfOneBatchAnswerInRequestOrder`, `TestRPCPromptsWithDueCompactionsAnswerInRequestOrder`: PiG's side on a saved Session, and with a compaction due).

Parity allowance: for two prompts of one chunk in RPC mode, the second prompt's response (`success:false` with the error against Pi's `started`), its place before the first prompt's `started`, the absence of Pi's extra `agent_settled`, and `isStreaming` and the answer to further prompts until the first run ends. The first prompt's response, its run and the settled Session are Pi's.

## D630 RPC mode reads no stdin inside a reload whose reloaded extension awaits while it loads

What: Pi's `session.reload()` awaits the resource loader after it has invalidated the old runner (`agent-session.ts:3825-3826`, `:3838`). When a reloaded extension's factory awaits a timer or I/O, Pi's event loop turns inside the reload and its RPC mode reads stdin there (`rpc-mode.ts:832-834`). A prompt written meanwhile is dispatched on the old runner: an extension command still resolves, its `ctx` throws the stale message (`runner.ts:729-741`), RPC writes `extension_error` and answers the prompt "handled" before the reload ends. Stdin's end read there exits 0 at once, and the command that awaited `ctx.reload()` never answers. PiG holds stdin from the reload's start to the continuation of the command that awaited it, also while a factory awaits: the prompt is read after the reload, runs on the reloaded extension and answers after the reloading command; at stdin's end the reload finishes, its command answers and the process exits.

Why: A prompt read inside the load can only fail: it runs on extensions the reload has already invalidated. Holding it gives the client the answer of the extension it reloaded, in the order it wrote its records, and never loses the reloading command's response. The host sees a loading factory's wait in no runtime today, and a Go, Python or Rust extension has no factory that awaits on the host's loop at all.

Pi bug: with a reloaded extension whose factory awaits while it loads, Pi's RPC mode reads stdin inside `reload()` (`rpc-mode.ts:832-834`) and runs a prompt written meanwhile on the runner the reload has invalidated (`agent-session.ts:3826`): an `extension_error` with the stale-context message and the prompt answered "handled"; at stdin's end it exits 0 and the response of the command that awaited `ctx.reload()` is never written. To be filed upstream.\n\nScope: `coding/cli/rpc_settle_gate.go` (`rpcSettleGate.holdReload`), `coding/cli/rpc_mode.go` (the reload the mode installs with `Runtime.SetReload`).

Owner decision: approved by the owner, 2026-11-13T00:54:22Z. The owner's words that day: "I want the divergences that keep us on brand but still want to be faithful as we can plus our additions of go support and then my hero demo I was asking for plus ensuring the optimal quality release given how I've held it back and we can make this an exciting announcment from many angles." The decision recorded from them at that time: "Divergences APPROVED where PiG keeps a user safe or told the truth (on brand), each numbered, minimal, with remove-when and an upstream issue draft: D630 (hold a prompt during a reload), D632 (serialised reloads), D633 (show a failed settings save), p2 guest per-turn authority (pending the lane's evidence that Pi does not limit guests elsewhere). Everything else stays faithful to Pi."
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern

Call-site markers: `coding/cli/rpc_settle_gate.go` (`holdReload`).

Evidence: Pi 1.5.2 with `coding/cli/testdata/rpc-reload.mjs` and `RL_LOAD=await`: a `/ping` written while the reloaded factory awaits gives `extension_error command:ping` with the stale message, `response c`, and only then `notify started g2`, `response b`; stdin's end there exits 0 with nothing written. PiG gives `notify started g2`, `response b`, `notify ping g2`, `response c` (`TestRPCPromptWrittenWhileAReloadedFactoryAwaitsRunsOnTheReloadedExtension`), and at stdin's end `notify started g2`, `response b`, exit 0 (`TestRPCReloadHoldEnds/stdin_ends_while_the_reloaded_factory_awaits`). A factory that does not await is the same in both: `TestRPCPromptWrittenDuringAReloadRunsOnTheReloadedExtension`.

Parity allowance: none.

Remove when: Pi stops reading stdin inside `reload()`, or rejects a prompt that arrives during it.

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:8107246dd1a1815b

## D631 A command run by `pi.exec` or the bash tool gets pipes for its output, where Pi gives it socket pairs

What: Pi starts a command with Node's `spawn` and `stdio: ["ignore", "pipe", "pipe"]` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/exec.ts:58-62`; the bash tool spawns the same way). On Linux and macOS libuv makes each such "pipe" a Unix socket pair (`uv_socketpair` in `uv__process_init_stdio`), so the command's stdout and stderr are sockets. PiG gives the command the write end of a pipe (`childwait.NewPipe`, `os.Pipe` on Unix). A command can tell the two apart, and two effects were measured (Linux container of the lane image, dash as `sh`, Pi 1.5.2 with the post-release backports, 96-CPU host xd6702):

1. Reopening its own output. `sh -c 'echo x > /dev/stdout'` through `execCommand`: Pi resolves code 2, stdout empty, stderr `sh: 1: cannot create /dev/stdout: No such device or address` (a socket cannot be opened through `/proc/self/fd/1`, ENXIO); PiG resolves code 0, stdout `x`. Every run, both sides. The same holds for `/dev/stderr` and `/proc/self/fd/N`, so for `tee /dev/stderr`.
2. The code of a command cut off at `maxBuffer` while it writes stdout. When the reader closes a socket that still holds unread data, the writer's next write fails once with ECONNRESET and raises no SIGPIPE; a pipe raises SIGPIPE at once. `sh -c 'exec yes'` over the cap: in Pi `yes` goes on to report `yes: standard output: Connection reset by peer` and exits 1 when it gets that far, is ended by Pi's SIGTERM while it reports (143), or its report meets the closed stderr (141). Pi's code was 1 in 257 of 3600 runs (7%) inside one Node process of 34800 calls and in 167 and 159 of 360 runs (46%, 44%) inside Node processes of 240 calls; of 1200 runs with stderr in a file, the 16 that ended 1 all held the full report and 664 of the 920 that ended 143 held its beginning. PiG's code was 141 in 3571 of 3600 runs and 143 in 29: never 1, and no report. Measured again on Linux 7.0 (tern-ci:v11 on xd670, lane ExecSigpipeFlake job m2, 200 calls per Node process): 11 of Pi's 20,000 runs alone and 3 of its 10,000 beside 32 busy loops reported `yes: standard output: Broken pipe` instead of the reset (code 143, once 1), because `unix_release_sock` marks the writer's end shut down (`net/unix/af_unix.c:707`) one statement before it posts ECONNRESET (`:709`), and a write that reads the two in between fails with EPIPE in `sock_alloc_send_pskb` (`net/core/sock.c:2979-2981`), which `unix_stream_sendmsg` returns without raising SIGPIPE (`af_unix.c:2431-2432`); PiG's 20,000 and 10,000 runs ended 141 or 143 and reported nothing.

Not measured: macOS. Expected there, as a guess: `/dev/stdout` is `/dev/fd/1`, which duplicates the descriptor instead of opening the file again, so effect 1 should not appear, and a write to a Unix socket whose peer closed gives EPIPE with SIGPIPE, so effect 2 should not appear either. Windows has named pipes on both sides and is not part of this record.


Measured at the Node level, not tested through the tools: `pi.exec` gives the command no stdin on either side (`"ignore"`, `/dev/null`). The bash tool gives it one only when the shell takes the command from stdin (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/tools/bash.ts:129`), and Node's is then a socket pair too. Measured once with Node's `spawn` and a `"pipe"` stdin: `stat -L -c %F /proc/self/fd/0` prints `socket` and `cat /dev/stdin` fails with `cat: /dev/stdin: No such device or address`; through a pipe, which is what PiG's bash tool gives the shell, the same command prints `fifo` and reads the input. It is not one of this record's effects until a test on each side holds it.

Why: a faithful port would make commands that work on Linux fail (`> /dev/stdout`, `tee /dev/stderr`), which no PiG user gains from. The cost of keeping pipes is that a command which fails under Pi for that reason succeeds under PiG, and that an exit code 1 which Pi shows in a share of cut-off runs never appears.

Pi bug: none. The socket pair is libuv's choice for every Node child; Pi does not ask for it.

Scope: `coding/extension` (`ExecCommand`, both output pipes) and `internal/codingagent/tools` (the bash tool's output pipe). Unix only.

Owner decision: approved. The owner said "proceed with recommendations" (2026-11-12T23:22:43Z, owner decisions of 0.8.0), and the recommendation recorded there for this item is "D631 exec/bash child stdio: KEEP PIPES, record D631 as a numbered divergence with both effects locked by tests against the Pi oracle."

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-12

Remove when: PiG gives the commands of `pi.exec` and the bash tool socket pairs on Unix (stdin included where the bash tool gives one), or Node gives its children pipes.

Call-site markers: `coding/extension/exec.go` (`ExecCommand`, the two `childwait.NewPipe` calls), `internal/codingagent/tools/bash_operations.go` (its `childwait.NewPipe` call).

Evidence: `coding/extension/exec_socket_linux_test.go` (`TestExecCommandReopensItsOutputWherePiCannot`: both sides' code and text for effect 1 through Pi's `execCommand`; `TestExecCommandCutOffWriterMeetsSIGPIPEWherePisMeetsAReset`: effect 2, PiG's writer never reports a write error and never exits 1, every run of Pi shows one of the three ends measured for it (`cutOffEnds`: a signal before any report, the reset report, the broken-pipe report), and Pi's writer that ignores SIGTERM reports the failed write), `coding/extension/exec_cutoff_ends_test.go` (`cutOffEnds` and `TestCutOffEndsAreTheOutcomesMeasuredForPi`: the code and stderr text of each end) and `coding/extension/exec_oracle_test.go` (`execSocketResetScript`: the runs of Pi's `exec yes` that reported a failed write are the only runs left out of the comparison of the codes, and a run of PiG that reports one is a fault).

Parity allowance: for the commands above PiG's code and output differ from Pi's as stated; every other field of an `ExecResult` and every other command of the exec oracle (`TestExecCommandMatchesPi`) is Pi's, and so are the codes of `exec yes` once the runs of Pi that reported a failed write are left out.

## D632 Resource loader operations that overlap wait for each other, where Pi's reloads interleave and can mix their results

What: Pi's `DefaultResourceLoader.reload()` (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/resource-loader.ts:541-710`) has no lock. A reload suspended at one of its awaits (the extension load, `:618`) lets a later `reload()` run to its end, and when the first resumes it assigns what it resolved before the await next to what it reads after it: the skill, prompt template and theme paths resolved before the extension load (`:585-590`, assigned at `:635`, `:650`, `:669`) and the context files and system prompt read after it (`:678-705`). A loader whose first reload finishes last therefore ends with the first reload's skills and the second reload's system prompt. PiG's `ReloadWith`, `LoadProjectTrustExtensions`, `ExtendResources` and `Close` take one operation lock (`opMu`): operations that overlap from outside wait for each other, each is whole, and the loader ends in the state of the operation that started last.

A call made from inside an operation runs inside it, as Pi's runs at once: a call on the operation's goroutine (an override function or any other callback that gets no context) and a call that carries the context the operation handed to its project-trust callback or extension load. That context marks the operation only while it runs; used after it, the call is one from outside and waits.

Why: Pi's interleaving is safe in one thread, where a reload gives up control only at an await. PiG's reloads run on goroutines, so two of them inside the loader at once would read and write its fields in parallel; the lock that prevents that also removes the mixed final state, which no caller can want.

Pi bug: not reported. Pi's mixed state after overlapping reloads looks unintended, but no upstream issue records it. A draft for the owner to file is in `docs/parity/gap-closure/ledger-coding-agent-pi-issue-drafts.md` (D632).

Scope: `coding` (`DefaultResourceLoader.ReloadWith`, `LoadProjectTrustExtensions`, `ExtendResources`, `Close`).

Limit: the lock does not cover a goroutine that a callback starts with its context and does not wait for. While the operation that handed out the context runs, a loader call that goroutine makes with it counts as made from inside the operation and runs beside the rest of it, without the lock. A callback that hands its context to another goroutine must wait for that goroutine before it returns, as a Pi callback awaits the reload it starts. Once the operation has ended the context marks nothing, and the call waits like any call from outside.

Owner decision: approved by the owner. `OWNER-DECISIONS.md`, section "2026-11-13T00:54:22Z owner: direction for the rest of 0.8.0", the owner verbatim: "I want the divergences that keep us on brand but still want to be faithful as we can plus our additions of go support and then my hero demo I was asking for plus ensuring the optimal quality release given how I've held it back and we can make this an exciting announcment from many angles." The line of that section that applies it, quoted: "Divergences APPROVED where PiG keeps a user safe or told the truth (on brand), each numbered, minimal, with remove-when and an upstream issue draft: D630 (hold a prompt during a reload), D632 (serialised reloads), D633 (show a failed settings save), ..."
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:d5e61a5d1abb6c9f

Remove when: Pi serialises `reload()` or orders its fields, so that overlapping reloads end in the state of one of them.

Call-site markers: `coding/resource_loader.go` (`takeOperation`).

Evidence: `coding/resource_loader_reentry_async_test.go` (`TestPiOverlappingReloadsMixTheirResultsD632` records Pi's mixed state through the oracle and fails when Pi stops mixing; `TestDefaultResourceLoaderOverrideMayCallTheLoader`, `TestDefaultResourceLoaderCallbackMayExtendAndClose`, `TestDefaultResourceLoaderCallbackMayReloadWithItsContextOnAnotherGoroutine`, `TestDefaultResourceLoaderContextOfAnEndedReloadDoesNotBypassTheOperationLock`) and `coding/resource_loader_overlap_async_modelsb_test.go` (`TestDefaultResourceLoaderOverlappingReloadsEndInTheLastInputsState`, `TestDefaultResourceLoaderManyOverlappingReloadsEndInTheSameState`, `TestDefaultResourceLoaderCallbackMayReloadWithItsContext`). Before the calls from inside an operation were let through, every one of them waited for the lock its own operation held: `TestDefaultResourceLoaderCallbackMayReloadWithItsContext` fails in its three cases without that, each at its 5 second bound.

Parity allowance: after reloads that overlap with different inputs PiG's loader holds the last reload's skills, prompt templates, themes, context files and system prompt, where Pi's can hold the earlier reload's paths next to the later one's prompt; with unchanged inputs both end in the same state.

## D633 A failed settings save is reported where the change was made, where Pi reports it only in its settings diagnostics

What: Pi's settings setters cannot fail: they change the value in memory and queue the write (`packages/coding-agent/src/core/settings-manager.ts:576-588`, `:635-642`), and a write that fails is recorded for `drainErrors()` (`:561-563`, `:674-678`). Pi's callers therefore go on as if the save had worked: the `/settings` callbacks apply the live effect (`modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:5154-5173`), `set_steering_mode` and `set_follow_up_mode` answer success (`modes/rpc/rpc-mode.ts:540-548`), `pi install` prints `Installed <source>` (`package-manager-cli.ts:1061-1063` over `core/package-manager.ts:1130-1133`), the config selector keeps the toggle (`modes/interactive/components/config-selector.ts:552-564`, `:626-628`) and an extension's `settingsManager.set...()` returns. The failure shows only where Pi drains the recorded errors: the settings diagnostics collected at startup and when a runtime is created (`core/settings-diagnostics.ts:5` from `main.ts:721` and `:860`) and the `Warning (<command>, <scope> settings): ...` lines the package and config commands print before they run (`package-manager-cli.ts:283-290`, `:926`, `:1038`).

PiG's setters perform their write before they return (the write queue is drained on the caller) and return its failure, besides recording it for `DrainErrors` as Pi does and keeping the change in memory as Pi does. Each caller below acts on the returned error at the point of the change:

- `/settings`: prints `Failed to save settings: <error>`, does not apply the change's live effect and shows the manager's value on the row again.
- The model selector of an interactive run without a Session: shows the error instead of the selection status.
- RPC `set_steering_mode` and `set_follow_up_mode`: answer an error response; the queue mode of the running agent has changed, as in Pi.
- `pig install` and `pig remove`: print the error and exit 1 after the package was installed or removed, where Pi prints `Installed` or `Removed` and exits 0. The package API of an extension (`addSourceToSettings`, `removeSourceFromSettings`) returns the error.
- `pig config`: prints `config toggle: <error>` and shows the entry in its previous state.
- An extension's `SettingsManager` setters and the Session operations `setSteeringMode`, `setFollowUpMode` and `setCacheWarmingMode`: return the error to the extension.
- `Session.SetCacheWarmingMode`: returns the error without telling the cache warmer that the mode changed. The mode in memory is the new one, as Pi keeps it, so the warmer's status reads the new mode; the call does not stop a refresh that is scheduled.

Internal, with nothing a user sees: `GetOrCreateDeviceID` keeps the new ID in memory when its save was skipped; the Session's default model, default thinking level, retry and auto-compaction setters, the changelog version setters, the enabled-models setter and the interactive cache-warming setter discard the returned error, as Pi's callers have none.

A setter called from inside a write of the same manager (a settings storage whose lock callback calls a setter) returns nil before its own write runs; its failure is recorded only.

Why: a user who changes a setting and is told nothing believes it is saved; on the next start the old value is back. Pi reports the failure at a later start, away from the change that caused it. PiG says so when and where the change was made.

Pi bug: not reported upstream yet. A draft for the owner to file is in `docs/parity/gap-closure/ledger-coding-agent-pi-issue-drafts.md` (D633).

Scope: `internal/codingagent` (`SettingsManager.UpdateGlobal`, `UpdateProject` and the setters over them; `settingsHandlerTUI`; `persistDefaultModel`; `GraphSettingsManager.Call`), `internal/packagemanager` (`AddSourceToSettings`, `RemoveSourceFromSettings`), `coding` (`Session.SetSteeringMode`, `SetFollowUpMode`, `SetCacheWarmingMode`) and `coding/cli` (RPC `set_steering_mode`, `set_follow_up_mode`; the config selector; the child session operations). The recorded error, the value kept in memory and the order of the writes are Pi's.

Owner decision: approved by the owner. `OWNER-DECISIONS.md`, section "2026-11-13T00:54:22Z owner: direction for the rest of 0.8.0", the owner verbatim: "I want the divergences that keep us on brand but still want to be faithful as we can plus our additions of go support and then my hero demo I was asking for plus ensuring the optimal quality release given how I've held it back and we can make this an exciting announcment from many angles." The line of that section that applies it, quoted: "Divergences APPROVED where PiG keeps a user safe or told the truth (on brand), each numbered, minimal, with remove-when and an upstream issue draft: D630 (hold a prompt during a reload), D632 (serialised reloads), D633 (show a failed settings save), ..."

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-13

Remove when: Pi reports a failed save at the point of change.

Call-site markers: `internal/codingagent/slash_session_handlers.go` (`settingsHandlerTUI`), `internal/codingagent/model_picker.go` (`persistDefaultModel`), `internal/codingagent/extension_settings_graph.go` (`GraphSettingsManager.Call`), `internal/packagemanager/package_manager.go` (`AddSourceToSettings`, `RemoveSourceFromSettings`), `coding/session.go` (`SetSteeringMode`, `SetFollowUpMode`), `coding/session_cache_warming.go` (`SetCacheWarmingMode`), `coding/cli/rpc_mode.go` (`set_steering_mode`, `set_follow_up_mode`), `coding/cli/config_command.go` (the selector's `OnToggle` and `OnOverride`) and `internal/codingagent/settings.go` (`updateGlobalReplacing`, `UpdateProject`).

Evidence: one test for each caller, in which every save fails (another holder has the settings lock; for `/settings`, a directory is in the file's place): `/settings` in `internal/codingagent/slash_session_handlers_test.go` (`TestSettingsHandlerTUI_FailedSaveRestoresTheRowValue`); the model selector and an extension's setters in `internal/codingagent/settings_failed_save_d633_test.go` (`TestModelSelectorShowsAFailedSaveOfTheDefaultModel`, `TestGraphSettingsManagerSettersReturnAFailedSave`); the Session operations in `coding/settings_failed_save_d633_test.go` (`TestSessionQueueModeSettersReturnAFailedSave`, `TestSetCacheWarmingModeReturnsAFailedSaveAndDoesNotTellTheWarmer`); RPC, `pig install`, the package removal and `pig config` in `coding/cli/settings_failed_save_d633_test.go` (`TestRPCQueueModeSettersAnswerAFailedSave`, `TestPackageInstallAndRemoveReportAFailedSave`, `TestConfigSelectorReportsAToggleThatCouldNotBeSaved`). The removal is proven at `RemoveAndPersist`, the call `pig remove` makes and reports: a lock held when the command starts also refuses the read of the package list, so the command itself finds nothing to remove. The setter itself: `internal/codingagent/file_lock_test.go` (`TestSettingsAndTrustLocksFailWhileHeld`, `TestSettingsLockTakesOverStaleLocks`, `TestSettingsAndTrustLockErrorsAreProperLockfileErrorsUpstream`, `TestSettingsUpdateKeepsMemoryWhenTheWriteFailsUpstream`: the setter returns proper-lockfile's ELOCKED error and the manager keeps the value) and `internal/codingagent/settings_write_queue_async_test.go` (`TestSettingsWriteFailureIsRecordedOnceAndTheQueueGoesOn`: returned and recorded once; `TestSettingsSetterCalledFromInsideAWriteReturnsNilAndItsFailureIsOnlyRecorded`). Pi's side is recorded through the oracle by `internal/codingagent/settings_write_queue_oracle_test.go` (`TestSettingsWriteQueueErrorPathMatchesPi`: Pi's setter does not throw the failed write and records it).

Parity allowance: after a settings write fails, PiG's output at the point of the change differs from Pi's (an error line or an error response where Pi shows success), the live effect of a `/settings` change and of `Session.SetCacheWarmingMode` is not applied, and `pig install` and `pig remove` exit 1; the settings in memory, the file, the recorded errors and the diagnostics Pi prints later are the same.

## D636 A page applies the language and the theme its user chose earlier when it opens, where Pi applies a stored pick only at its next change

What: Pi's followers of the user's locale choice and theme choice only listen. `followActiveLanguage` (`packages/chord/src/surfaces/active-language.ts:57-66`) and `followActiveTheme` (`packages/chord/src/surfaces/active-theme.ts:43-52`) hold the choice and run `choice.pick.watch(ctx).then((watch) => watch.start(...))`: `pick` is set in a listener call and `watch.value` is never read. A member's watch of a hold opens at the hold's first values (`packages/chord/src/host/reference.ts:592-599`: `await firstValues`, then `source.openWatch`) and holds that moment's value as the one it was acquired with (`packages/chord/src/host/reference-source.ts:112-117`); a watch's listener gets the changes after it, never the value the watch started at (`packages/chord/src/watch.ts:44-50`: `value` is the "acquisition revision before start", and `start` never invokes the listener inline). So a pick the server stored before the client's hold had its first values is never applied: a page that opens or reloads shows the device's language and the first theme of its mode until the user chooses again. Observed on Pi's own server and page (the mirror at the pin, Chrome for Testing 156): with `ar` stored before the page opens the root element stays `lang="en"`, and with a theme stored before it opens the page shows the first theme of its mode; a later choice is followed in both, and a pick chosen one second after the page connected is applied. The files are the same blobs at Pi main `d36266e06669`. PiG's followers apply the value their watch was acquired with before they start its listener: the vendored TypeScript followers through the patch `automation/gen/chord-patches/D636-apply-acquired-pick.patch` (in both vendored chord trees), and the Go ports `FollowActiveLanguage` and `FollowActiveTheme`.

Why: the choice is stored for each principal so that every page and terminal of that user shows it; a choice that lasts until the next reload reads as a setting that does not save. The same window made Pi's own page test of the language (`packages/chord/test/node/web/language.test.ts`, ported as `TestThePagesLanguageWritesLangAndDirOnTheRootElementFromTheDeviceThenTheUsersPick`) fail 3 runs in 100 here: it chooses as soon as the page rendered and loses when the server stores the choice before it fixes the first values of the page's hold. Waiting in the test until the server lists the page's reference does not close the window (6 runs in 200 still failed): only applying the acquired value does. This is a cure of a Pi defect under the owner's rule of 2026-11-13T22:52:04Z ("fix all bugs"), not a difference forced by Go.

Pi bug: yes. Both followers should apply `watch.value` before `watch.start` (`pick = watch.value; update();`).

Scope: `chord/nextnode/surfaces/client/pi/chord/src/surfaces/active-language.ts` and `active-theme.ts`, the same two files under `chord/nextnode/plugins/node/pi/chord/src/surfaces`, and `chord/next/surfaces` (`FollowActiveLanguage`, `FollowActiveTheme`). An app's own vendored copy of Pi's chord (the p2 web bundle's) is not changed by this record's patch.

Owner decision: owner, 2026-11-13T03:33:12Z: "We replace pi with pig everywhere bro and for anything you think we can make a meaningful improvement to the experience do it", recorded as "Meaningful experience improvements are APPROVED as numbered divergences, each with a test and a changelog line: share-dialog warning + plain text on Read/write links; Familiar gets an off switch; chord/p2 pages apply the stored language/theme at load; backup answers (refuse wider pre-existing folder naming the chmod; write-only targets allowed and said; remote targets need a key unless 'plain' is set; secrets only from a file; Radius plain gzip disclosed) + rclone and age targets."; and owner, 2026-11-13T03:48:42Z: "Proceed with all recommendations."
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:9af83eb72870d1ac

Remove when: Pi's followers apply the value their watch was acquired with. The patch then no longer applies at the re-vendor: drop it with this record and the markers, and keep the Go ports as the port of Pi's lines. Or the owner withdraws the change.

Call-site markers: `chord/nextnode/surfaces/client/pi/chord/src/surfaces/active-language.ts` and `active-theme.ts`, `chord/nextnode/plugins/node/pi/chord/src/surfaces/active-language.ts` and `active-theme.ts` (the patch's lines), `chord/next/surfaces/surfaces_active_language.go` (`FollowActiveLanguage`), `chord/next/surfaces/surfaces_active_theme.go` (`FollowActiveTheme`).

Evidence: `chord/nextnode/app/app_web_stored_pick_test.go` (`TestThePagesLanguageFollowsAPickStoredBeforeThePageOpens`, `TestAPageAppliesAThemeStoredBeforeItOpens`, in a real Chromium; both fail on Pi's files), `chord/next/surfaces/surfaces_active_language_test.go` (`TestFollowsAPickThePrincipalStoredBeforeTheFollowerStarted`), `chord/next/surfaces/surfaces_active_theme_test.go` (`TestFollowsAThemePickThePrincipalStoredBeforeTheFollowerStarted`), and `chord/nextnode/app/app_web_browser_test.go` (`TestThePagesLanguageWritesLangAndDirOnTheRootElementFromTheDeviceThenTheUsersPick`, Pi's test as written, which no longer loses to the window).

Parity allowance: a page or a Go follower whose user stored a pick before it started shows that pick once its hold of the choice binds, where Pi shows the device's language and the first theme of the mode until the pick's next change; with no stored pick, and for every change after the bind, what is shown and when is Pi's.

## D645 A name that is an `Object.prototype` member is no entry of eighteen Stock lookups, where Pi's plain objects answer with the inherited member

What: Pi keeps the tables of these eighteen lookups as plain JavaScript objects and reads them with `table[name]` or tests them with `name in table`. A plain object inherits the members of `Object.prototype`, so for a name such as `constructor`, `toString`, `valueOf`, `hasOwnProperty`, `isPrototypeOf` or `__proto__` the lookup does not answer "no entry": it returns the inherited function (or, for `__proto__`, the prototype object), and the code that follows treats that value as the entry it asked for. PiG keeps the same tables as Go maps (and, for the MCP overrides, as an ordered list of own entries), which have no inherited members: such a name has no entry, exactly like any other name the table does not hold. The name comes from outside in every row: the math of a model's answer, a chunk of terminal input, a theme file, the name an MCP server gives a tool, the name of a selected tool (rows 1 to 5); the name of an MCP server and an option on the `mcp` command line, a key of `keybindings.json`, a theme name, an environment reference in an API key or a header, a provider id, a key of a project settings file, a provider of a legacy `apiKeys` entry (rows 6 to 15). In rows 6, 7 and 14 Pi's code also writes with `table[name] = value`, which for the name `__proto__` sets the table's prototype and makes no entry. Rows 16 to 18 were added on 2026-11-14: the file extension behind the read and write cards (Pi's table, row 16), and two tables of libraries Pi runs on: TypeBox's property test and value hash (row 17) and Node's `TERM` table behind the evals report (row 18).

Each cell below was run on 2026-11-13 with Node 24.22.2. Pi 1.5.2 is the pinned build (b4afdddf44e0): the call was made once against the oracle build of the pin and once against the compiled Pi the repository vendors (`coding/extension/host/subprocess/runtime-node/shims/pi-dist`, what the pinning tests load), and the 37 answers of the two were equal. Pi 275dde410065 is a source build of Pi's main of 2026-11-13: the same 37 calls gave the same 37 answers. PiG's cell is what the pinning test asserts. The command line sentences of rows 3, 4 and 5 were run on the same day with the oracle build of the pin and PiG's parity binary, the test provider on both sides, three runs per side with equal output: rows 4 and 5 in print mode, row 3 in a 100x35 terminal. The modules are `packages/tui/dist/index.js` (`renderLatex`, `parseKey`), `packages/coding-agent/dist/modes/interactive/theme/theme.js` (`loadThemeFromPath`), `dist/core/mcp-servers.js` (`getMcpToolExposure`) and `dist/core/system-prompt.js` (`buildSystemPrompt`). Rows 6 to 15, and the `mcp list` sentence of row 4, were run on 2026-11-13 with Node 24.22.2 against the oracle build of the pin (function calls, and for rows 6, 7 and 13 the command line of that build against PiG's parity binary, each in scratch directories) and are run against the vendored build by their pinning tests. They were not run at Pi 275dde410065 nor at any later Pi: their fourth column says what was read in the source of Pi c23b9ec1ef12 (2026-11-07).

| Site: Pi's lookup, PiG's lookup | Call and names | Pi 1.5.2 | Pi 275dde410065 | PiG | Pinning test |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Latex symbol. Pi `packages/tui/src/latex.ts:1090` `SYMBOLS[command]`, `:1075` `NEGATED_SYMBOLS[value]`. PiG `internal/latex/latex.go` `latexSymbols()[command]`, `latexNegatedSymbols()[value]`. | `renderLatex(source)` for `\constructor`, `x \toString y`, `\valueOf`, `\hasOwnProperty`, `\isPrototypeOf`, `\not{constructor}`, `\not{toString}` | The function's source text is drawn as the symbol: `function Object() { [native code] }`, `x function toString() { [native code] } y`, `function valueOf() { [native code] }`, `function hasOwnProperty() { [native code] }`, `function isPrototypeOf() { [native code] }`; `\not{constructor}` gives `function Object() { [native code] }` and `\not{toString}` gives `function toString() { [native code] }` | the same seven answers | The five commands are no symbol and the expression is not converted (`RenderLatex` answers `ok == false`, which is what both sides answer for `\nosuchsymbol`). `\not{constructor}` gives `c̸onstructor` and `\not{toString}` gives `t̸oString`: the first character struck, as for any argument that is not in the table. | `internal/latex` `TestACommandNamedLikeAnObjectPrototypeMemberIsNoLatexSymbol` |
| 2. Key id. Pi `packages/tui/src/keys.ts:1376` `LEGACY_SEQUENCE_KEY_IDS[data]`. PiG `tui/key_parse.go` `parsedLegacySequenceKeyIDs[data]`. | `parseKey(data)` for the one chunk `constructor`, `toString`, `valueOf`, `hasOwnProperty`, `__proto__` | The functions `Object`, `toString`, `valueOf`, `hasOwnProperty` and, for `__proto__`, the object `{}`, where a key id string is promised | the same five answers | No key (`ParseKey` answers `ok == false`, which is what both sides answer for `nosuchkey`) | `tui` `TestAChunkNamedLikeAnObjectPrototypeMemberIsNoKey` |

Why: these are defects of Pi, and PiG does not port a defect. None of Pi's answers in rows 1 to 5 is something a user or a caller can use: the text of a native function drawn inside a formula, a function returned as a key id, a `TypeError` in place of the loader's own message, a function returned as an exposure, and a system prompt that cannot be built because a tool has a certain name. Reproducing them would mean adding the members of `Object.prototype` to the Go tables by hand. Rows 6 to 15 are the same defect in ten more places. In rows 6, 7 and 15 PiG reproduced Pi's answer on purpose until 2026-11-13, with a table of the `Object.prototype` names: it reported a new server as replaced, wrote no server named `__proto__`, and renamed the user's keybinding key to the text of a native function. None of that serves a user, so those tables are gone and PiG answers as for any other name. Rows 16 to 18 are defects of the same kind: a generic fallback card in place of the write and read cards of a file, an object rejected because it omits an optional property, two different objects counted as one item, and a report title that loses its bold.

Pi bug: yes, at Pi 1.5.2 and at Pi 275dde410065. For a name that is a member of `Object.prototype`, Pi draws the source text of a native function inside a formula, returns a function where a key id is promised, reports `TypeError: value.startsWith is not a function` for a theme color, does not offer an MCP tool of a `direct` server, and ends print mode with `function is not iterable` before any turn when an extension tool has such a name. Pi has no test for such a name at any of the five sites. Rows 6 to 15, at Pi 1.5.2 (the lookups read the same in the source of Pi c23b9ec1ef12): `mcp add constructor` reports a new server as replaced and `mcp add __proto__` writes no server; a `keybindings.json` key `constructor` is renamed to `function Object() { [native code] }` in the user's file; a theme named `constructor` fails with `Cannot convert undefined or null to object`; `$constructor` in an API key or a header resolves to the text of a native function and is sent; `readStoredCredential("constructor")` returns a function; `findEnvKeys("constructor")` throws `TypeError: envVars.filter is not a function`; a user keybinding named `constructor` is reported in a conflict; `mcp add srv --constructor v` is accepted; a project settings key `__proto__` sets settings through the prototype of the merged object; the legacy API key of a provider named `constructor` is not moved to `auth.json`; and `mcp list` prints `constructor [function Object() { [native code] }]`. A file named `notes.constructor` or `x.__proto__` gets the generic fallback card in place of its write and read cards (row 16). Rows 17 and 18 are defects of TypeBox 1.3.27 and of Node 24, not of Pi's code.

Owner decision: the owner's line of 2026-11-13T03:33:12Z (`## 2026-11-13T03:33:12Z owner: brand + experience`, quoted in D483 as "We replace pi with pig everywhere bro and for anything you think we can make a meaningful improvement to the experience do it"). Its application to these five sites was relayed in the project channel on 2026-11-13T08:57:55Z, verbatim: "YES: the five Stock prototype-lookup sites are covered by the owner's 03:33:12Z line: PiG does not port those defects; one class record + a row and pinning test per site; add the same class elsewhere to the private upstream draft." Rows 6 to 15 are under the owner's rule of 2026-11-13T22:52:04Z as it was relayed to the lanes: every bug found is fixed and not only recorded, and a Pi bug that PiG can cure is cured, invisibly to Pi interop where possible, else as a numbered divergence with an upstream draft; the rule names "the constructor/__proto__ class EVERYWHERE" as such a bug. One exception was ruled on 2026-11-13 and is not a row: a `__proto__` member of streamed tool arguments stays dropped, as Pi's parser drops it (`ai/streaming_json.go`), because it must not reach extension JavaScript as an own key.
By owner principle (decision 29, 2026-11-17), checked by Claude and Tern; rows 16 to 18 under the owner's fix-all-bugs rule of 2026-11-13T22:52:04Z

SCRUTINIZED:approved 2026-11-17 text-sha256:b78e167a4ed2221c (for the class: rows 1 to 5 by the YES of 2026-11-13T08:57:55Z quoted above, rows 6 to 18 by the owner's rule of 2026-11-13T22:52:04Z)

Scope: exactly the eighteen rows of the table, in Stock (`internal/latex`, `tui`, `coding/extension`, `internal/codingagent/prompts`, `coding/mcpext`, `internal/codingagent`, `internal/configvalue`, `coding`, `ai`, `internal/typebox`, `internal/evals`), and for row 14 the Node extension runtime's `pi.getSettings()`. Not in this record:

- p2: the terminal client's fence and path lookup is D483 and the page's is D644.
- A lookup that was run and is equal: the HTML export template's `getLanguageFromPath` (PiG ships Pi's function text).
- Places where PiG still answers as Pi does for such a name, on purpose: the codemode store key `__proto__` (`codemode/execution.go`: the stored keys become members of JSON objects that extensions and Pi read, the `codemode-store` session entry and the durable store document), the type engine's `key in value` for the Go, Rust, Python and Lisp SDKs (`internal/typebox/engine/value.go`: the schema an extension registers is sent to the provider and must be the one TypeBox builds in the Node SDK, the same for every SDK), and the `__proto__` member of streamed tool arguments (`ai/streaming_json.go`), which stays as it is by the ruling quoted under Owner decision.
- Lookups that PiG answers by own entry with no visible difference from Pi: the highlight theme's scope lookup (`tui/highlight.go`; highlight.js's `reasonml` grammar emits the scope `constructor`, and in the theme `highlightCode` builds both lookups draw the same bytes, which `TestHighlightMatchesPiOracle` compares for every grammar), and a mermaid class named `__proto__` (`internal/mermaid`: lovely-mermaid draws the class's style, because the class becomes its table's prototype and the lookup reads it back, and PiG draws the same bytes, `TestAClassNamedProtoIsDrawnAsLovelyMermaidDrawsIt`).
- Tool-argument validation: Pi's validator tests own properties and so does PiG's; both report a required `constructor`, `toString`, `__proto__` or `hasOwnProperty` as missing and count a member named `constructor` in `uniqueItems` (`ai` `TestAToolArgumentNamedLikeAnObjectPrototypeMemberIsValidatedAsAnOwnProperty`). It does not go through the code of row 17, which only `internal/typebox/engine/value_check.go` imports.
- Pi's own files that PiG carries byte for byte: the compiled Pi that extensions load in the Node runtime (`shims/pi-dist`) answers as Pi does for every row, for an extension that calls Pi's exported helpers itself.
- Lookups of Pi that reach `Object.prototype` and were run with an equal visible answer on both sides: `\begin{constructor}` (unsupported on both sides), an MCP `exposure` of `constructor` (the same validation error), a codemode `$ref` of `#/constructor` (unknown), an extension shortcut named like a member (`extensions/runner.ts:131, 696`: the inherited function has no `restrictOverride`, read), and a model id `constructor` beside `modelOverrides` of another id (`--list-models` prints the same rows).
- Lookups of Pi that reach `Object.prototype` and that no probe of this record reached: `core/export-html/index.ts:211` `renderedTools[toolCallId]` (needs a session whose provider gave a tool call the id `constructor` and an HTML export on both sides); `modes/interactive/interactive-mode.ts:367, 6301` `defaultModelPerProvider[providerId]` (reached only after an interactive `/login` of a provider so named); `packages/ai/src/api/system-one-shared.ts:165` `ASSET_FORMATS[image.mimeType]` (needs the System One service and an image whose type is `constructor`); `core/provider-composer.ts:671, 683` `extensionImages[model.api]` (needs an image or classifier model whose api is so named). A provider whose `api` is `constructor` was run in print mode: Pi answers `No API provider registered for api: constructor` and PiG `Connection error.: bad port`; whether an ordinary unknown api name differs the same way was not run, so this is not a row.
- The Go extension SDK's language lookup (`extensions/sdk/language.go`), which was read and not run.

The record does not claim that these are all such lookups in Pi. They were found at Pi 275dde410065 by listing the tables declared as `const NAME: Record<string, ...> = {` in `packages/coding-agent/src`, `packages/tui/src`, `apps/p2/src` and `apps/p2/plugins` (64 tables) and keeping those read as `NAME[<variable>]` with no `Object.hasOwn(NAME, ...)` or `in NAME` beside the read (30 tables). That search cannot see a table declared another way, an object built at run time (a `{}` default, a parsed JSON or config object, an options argument), an `in` test that is itself the defect, an `Object.keys` loop, or a `Map`. Rows 3, 4 and 5 are objects built at run time and came from reading call sites, not from the search. Tables the search listed and nobody ran: coding-agent `IMAGE_EXTENSIONS`, `PI_LOGO_COLORS`, `TERMUX_PACKAGES`, `TOOLS`, `properties`; pi-tui `ACCENTS`, `BLACKBOARD`, `PATH_WRAPPERS`.

Remove when: Pi looks these names up as own properties (or with a `Map`) at the site; each row can then go alone, with its marker, and the record is retired with the last row.
